for type IIB and
t_{+}^{\mu}\bar{t}_{-}^{\nu}
for type IIA.

5.1.2
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansions within the
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
degeneration

We shall now argue that our proposal (
3.1
) and (
3.2
) for one-loop matrix
elements arises naturally when expanding the
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
degeneration of one-loop
string amplitudes in
\alpha^{\prime}
. In comparison to the full loop integrand of string amplitudes obtained
from performing the
z_{j}
- and
\tau
-integrals in (
5.1
) and (
5.4
), the

\tau\rightarrow i\infty
degeneration is insensitive to terms
without any massless propagators
\ell^{-2}
or
(\ell+K)^{-2}
which result in rational loop integrals.
For open strings, the dominant contributions from the degeneration are most conveniently
found by studying the
\tau
-integrand of (
5.4
) in terms of the quantity

F(\ell,\tau)=\int_{\mathfrak{C}_{\tau}(\gamma)}\mathrm{d}z_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}z_{n}\,e^{-2\pi i\alpha^{\prime}\tau\ell^{2}}\,{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\,{\cal K}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\,,

(5.11)

and by inserting
0=-F(\ell,i\infty)+F(\ell,i\infty)
into

\displaystyle A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}(1,2,\ldots,n)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\int_{0}^{i\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,e^{2\pi i\alpha^{\prime}\tau\ell^{2}}F(\ell,\tau)

(5.12)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\int_{0}^{i\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,e^{2\pi i\alpha^{\prime}\tau\ell^{2}}\Big\{\underbrace{F(\ell,\tau)-F(\ell,i\infty)}_{(i)}+\underbrace{F(\ell,i\infty)}_{(ii)}\Big\}\,.

The factor of
e^{-2\pi i\alpha^{\prime}\tau\ell^{2}}
in (
5.11
) is engineered to cancel the
\ell^{2}
-dependence of
{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)
in (
5.8
) and to ensure that
F(\ell,\tau)
is independent on
\ell^{2}
.
The first part
(i)
in (
5.12
) is suppressed at the cusp since
F(\ell,\tau)-F(\ell,i\infty)
vanishes
as
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
. In the second part
(ii)
, one can directly perform the
\tau
integral

\int_{0}^{i\infty}\mathrm{d}\tau\,e^{2\pi i\alpha^{\prime}\tau\ell^{2}}=\frac{i}{2\pi\alpha^{\prime}\ell^{2}}

(5.13)

under the assumption that
\ell^{2}
has a positive real part to drop the contribution from
the integration limit
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
. The accompanying limit
F(\ell,i\infty)

can be simplified by means of the results in section
5.1.1

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\rightarrow i\infty}F(\ell,\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n-1}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\mathfrak{D}_{j}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{3}\ldots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{n}}{\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}\ldots\sigma_{n}}\lim_{\tau\rightarrow i\infty}e^{-2\pi i\alpha^{\prime}\tau\ell^{2}}\,{\cal K}_{n}(\ell,\tau){\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{3}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\mathfrak{D}_{j}}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}\,\ldots\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{n}\,{\cal I}_{n}(\ell)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{j+}^{2{\alpha^{\prime}}\ell\cdot k_{j}}\prod_{1\leq i<j}^{n}|\sigma_{ij}|^{2{\alpha^{\prime}}k_{i}\cdot k_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{(2\pi i)^{3}(\alpha^{\prime})^{n-1}}\sum_{\rho\in S_{n}}N_{+|\rho(12\ldots n)|-}(\ell)

(5.14)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \times\lim_{k_{\pm}\rightarrow\pm\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{n}Z(+,j,j{-}1,\ldots,2,1,n,\ldots,j{+}1,-|+,\rho(1,2,\ldots,n),-)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{(2\pi i)^{3}(\alpha^{\prime})^{n-1}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{\rm eff}(+,j,j{-}1,\ldots,2,1,n,\ldots,j{+}1,-)\,,

based on the limits (
5.7
), (
5.8
) and (
5.9
).
The integration domains
\mathfrak{D}_{j}
defined in (
5.7
) are the disk orderings
of the
Z
-integrals identified in the third step, and we have expanded the kinematic
half integrand in terms of Parke-Taylor factors and
n
-gon numerators
N_{+|\rho|-}

according to (
2.8
).

In summary, by combining (
5.12
), (
5.13
) and (
5.14
), the
part
(ii)
of one-loop open-string amplitudes that carries the dominant contribution
at the cusp coincides with the proposal (
3.1
) for one-loop matrix elements,

A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}(1,2,\ldots,n)\,\big|_{(ii)}=\frac{A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(n,n{-}1,\ldots,2,1)}{16\pi^{4}({\alpha^{\prime}})^{n}}=\frac{A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,\ldots,n)}{16\pi^{4}(-{\alpha^{\prime}})^{n}}\,.

(5.15)

In particular, the
\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
limit of (
5.14
) reduces
Z\rightarrow m

and extracts the representation (
2.12
) of SYM amplitudes from
conventional strings, complementing its earlier derivation from ambitwistor strings.

The other part

(i)
of the one-loop open-string amplitude in (
5.12
) does not
have the pole in
\ell^{2}
which enters

(ii)
via (
5.13
). Since the quantity

F(\ell,\tau)
only depends on
\ell\cdot k_{j}
but not on

\ell^{2}
, there is no chance for

A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}(1,2,\ldots,n)\,\big|_{(i)}
to have a massless quadratic propagator
(\ell+K)^{-2}
. Upon loop integration, part

(i)
is therefore free of branch cuts in the Mandelstam invariants
corresponding to massless particles
and belongs to the analytic part of the one-loop open-string
amplitude.

A similar decomposition can be performed for the one-loop
closed-string amplitude (
5.1
). As detailed in appendix

B
, the proposal (
3.2
) for gravitational
one-loop matrix elements can be motivated from the dominant
contributions at
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
. Note that the absolute normalization of the string
amplitudes (
5.1
) and (
5.4
) has not been tracked;
it can be determined from unitarity or S-duality of type-IIB
superstrings.

5.1.3
Universal one-loop matrix elements from torus compactifications

In preparation for one-loop string amplitudes in supersymmetry-preserving toroidal compactifications,
we have already adapted the loop integration measure
\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell
in (
5.1
) and (
5.4
)
to a generic number
D
of spacetime dimensions. Upon compactification on a
10{-}D
torus,
open-string integrands additionally feature a
\tau
-dependent partition function to account for
Kaluza-Klein modes, and closed-string integrands involve a similar partition function encoding
both Kaluza-Klein and winding modes
Green:1982sw
;
Green:1987mn
. Both types of
partition functions reduce to a constant in the limit
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
relevant to one-loop matrix
elements in (
5.15
) and (
B.6
). By absorbing the
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
degeneration
of the partition functions into the normalization, the loop integrands of the matrix elements
(
3.1
) and (
3.2
) turn out to be dimension agnostic under torus compactification
of the underlying string theory. This is consistent with the construction of the one-loop matrix
elements from forward limits in the massless states of tree amplitudes, without any Kaluza-Klein
or winding modes in the propagators.

5.2
Quadratic propagators from homology invariance

The ambitwistor approach to SYM and supergravity amplitudes does
not manifest that the linearized propagators from the Parke-Taylor integrals
(
2.9
) eventually recombine to quadratic ones. In our proposal (
3.1
)
and (
3.2
), the translation between
linearized and quadratic propagators

Gomez:2016cqb
;
Gomez:2017lhy
;
Gomez:2017cpe
;
Ahmadiniaz:2018nvr
;
Agerskov:2019ryp
;
Farrow:2020voh

has to be performed order by order in the
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-expansion of the
Z
- and
J
integrals reviewed in section

2.3
. This procedure led to the four- and five-point results in section
4
, and there is no
fundamental obstruction to higher-point extensions. Still, higher and higher orders in
{\alpha^{\prime}}
introduce a growing number of factors
\ell\cdot k_{j}
into the numerators, so the efforts to find quadratic-propagator representations will grow accordingly by the shifts of loop momenta in the second step of (
2.17
) or in (
2.18
).

We shall now discuss a condition on amplitude building blocks to admit a quadratic-propagator representation.
In particular, we pinpoint the smallest contributions to the half integrands
{\cal I}_{n}(\ell)
which can integrate
to quadratic propagators and thereby identify a particularly economic organization scheme to obtain them
from linearized propagators. On the one hand, having an integration measure
\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm tree}
of
forward-limit form in (
2.2
) is
tied to the degeneration
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
. On the other hand, the salient point for compatibility with
quadratic propagators will be the behaviour of the chiral integrands
under translations
z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+\tau
around the
\mathfrak{B}
-cycle which can only be meaningfully studied at finite
\tau
.

The torus integrals in one-loop closed-string amplitudes (
5.1
) are only well-defined for
doubly-periodic integrands under
z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+1
and
z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+\tau

for all of
j=1,2,\ldots,n
. By the integral over
\ell\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
,
it will be sufficient to attain double-periodicity up to shifts of the loop momentum. By momentum
conservation and the quasi-periodicity
\theta_{1}(z{+}\tau,\tau)=-e^{-i\pi\tau-2\pi iz}\theta_{1}(z,\tau)
,
one can easily check the chiral Koba-Nielsen factor (
5.2
) to exhibit the following
\mathfrak{A}
-
and
\mathfrak{B}
-cycle monodromies
DHoker:1989cxq

\displaystyle{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\big|_{z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+1}

\displaystyle=e^{4\pi i{\alpha^{\prime}}\ell\cdot k_{j}}{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\,,

\displaystyle{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\big|_{z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+\tau}

\displaystyle={\cal J}_{n}(\ell{+}k_{j},\tau)\,.

(5.16)

The phases under the
\mathfrak{A}
-cycle shift
z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+1

drop out after combining the Koba-Nielsen factors (
5.2
) of both chiral halves,
so their contributions to the closed-string amplitude (
5.1
) have the
required doubly-periodicity.
19
19

19

While the prescription (
5.1
)
with
|{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)|^{2}
in the integrand is tailored to real kinematic invariants

\ell^{2},\ell\cdot k_{j}
and
k_{i}\cdot k_{j}
,
one can reconcile homology invariance with complex kinematics by employing
-k_{j}^{\ast},-\ell^{\ast}

instead of
k_{j},\ell
in one of the chiral halves. Still, the loop integral in (
5.1
) only converges
for real kinematics after Wick rotating the zeroth component of both
\ell
and
k_{j}
.

In order to have the same doubly-periodicity properties for type-II and heterotic one-loop amplitudes,
the left- and right-moving chiral correlators
{\cal K}_{n}(\ell)
in (
5.1
) need to separately satisfy
DHoker:1989cxq

\displaystyle{\cal K}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\big|_{z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+1}

\displaystyle={\cal K}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\,,

\displaystyle{\cal K}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\big|_{z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}+\tau}

\displaystyle={\cal K}_{n}(\ell{+}k_{j},\tau)\,.

(5.17)

Functions of
z_{j},\ell,k_{j},\tau
that obey (
5.17
) were dubbed
generalized elliptic integrands

and systematically constructed in
Mafra:2017ioj
;
Mafra:2018pll
;
Mafra:2018qqe
. We will attribute
the shifts on both sides of (
5.17
) to the action of the homology group on
z_{j},\ell
and follow the terminology of
DHoker:2020prr
:

E(z_{j},\ell,\tau)=E(z_{j}{+}\tau,\ell{-}k_{j},\tau)\ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ E\ \text{is referred to as {\it homology invariant}}\,.

(5.18)

Clearly, elliptic (i.e. meromorphic and doubly-periodic) functions of
z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{n}
are automatically homology invariant.
Moreover, as becomes pressing for
(n\geq 6)
-point examples, the notion of homology invariance extends
to the situation where (
5.18
) holds up to total Koba-Nielsen derivatives in the
z_{j}
,
20
20

20

For instance,
the following quantity relevant to six-point correlators
Mafra:2017ioj
;
Mafra:2018pll
;
Mafra:2018qqe
is
homology invariant in the sense of (
5.19
) but does not obey the stronger condition (
5.18
),

E_{1|2|3,4,5,6}=-2s_{12}g^{(2)}(z_{12},\tau)+\bigg(2\pi i\ell\cdot k_{2}+\sum_{j=3}^{6}s_{2j}\partial_{z_{2}}\log\theta_{1}(z_{2j},\tau)\bigg)\partial_{z_{1}}\log\theta_{1}(z_{12},\tau)\,.

The first term features
g^{(2)}(z,\tau)=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z,\tau))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\wp(z,\tau)
, a meromorphic Kronecker–Eisenstein coefficient ubiquitous to the

(n\geq 6)
-point correlators in the references involving the Weierstraß function
\wp(z,\tau)
.

\displaystyle\int\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}z_{i}\bigg){\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)E(z_{j},\ell,\tau)=\int\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}z_{i}\bigg){\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)E(z_{j}{+}\tau,\ell{-}k_{j},\tau)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \Longrightarrow\ \ \ E\ \text{is referred to as {\it homology invariant}}\,.

(5.19)

The same types of shifts
\ell\rightarrow\ell{-}k_{j}
arise in the sufficient condition (
2.20
)
for numerators of generic
(p{\leq}n)
-gon diagrams in the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansions to admit a
quadratic-propagator representation. Indeed, the associated
cyclic relabellings
\mathfrak{N}_{+|\ldots j|-}\leftrightarrow\mathfrak{N}_{+|j\ldots|-}
of the numerators
in (
2.20
) descend from the
shift
z_{j}\rightarrow z_{j}{+}\tau
around the
\mathfrak{B}
-cycle. This is a first piece of evidence
that homology invariance has important implications for the appearance of quadratic propagators.

5.2.1
Five-point examples

The four-point correlator
{\cal K}_{4}
in (
5.10
) is independent on
z_{j}
and
\ell

and thereby trivially a homology invariant by (
5.18
).
The simplest non-trivial examples of homology invariants occur in
the one-loop five-point correlator
Mafra:2017ioj
;
Mafra:2018qqe

\displaystyle E_{1|23,4,5}

\displaystyle=\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z_{12},\tau)+\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z_{23},\tau)+\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z_{31},\tau)

(5.20)

\displaystyle E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}

\displaystyle=2\pi i\ell^{\mu}+\big[k_{2}^{\mu}\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z_{12},\tau)+(2\leftrightarrow 3,4,5)\big]\,.

(5.21)

Given the transformations
\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z{\pm}1,\tau)=\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z,\tau)

as well as
\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z{\pm}\tau,\tau)=\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z,\tau)\mp 2\pi i
, the
first example (
5.20
) is elliptic and therefore homology invariant by virtue of the
cyclic sum of theta functions. For the
vectorial function in (
5.21
), homology invariance w.r.t.
z_{j}
is tied to the interplay of

\ell
with the monodromies of the theta functions and in case of
z_{1}
requires five-point
momentum conservation
k_{12345}=0
. Based on the kinematic identity (
4.38
) for

(k_{j})_{\mu}t_{+}^{\mu}(1,2,3,4,5)
, the five-point correlator in (
5.10
) can be written in manifestly
homology-invariant form as
Mafra:2017ioj
;
Mafra:2018qqe

{\cal K}_{5}(\ell)=(E_{1|2,3,4,5})_{\mu}t_{+}^{\mu}(1,2,3,4,5)+\big[E_{1|23,4,5}t_{8}(23,1,4,5)+(2,3|2,3,4,5)\big]\,,

(5.22)

where
+(2,3|2,3,4,5)
instructs to add five inequivalent permutations of
E_{1|23,4,5}t_{8}(23,1,4,5)

obtained from
23\leftrightarrow 24,25,34,35,45
.
21
21

21

This selection of permutations can be understood
from the properties
E_{1|23,4,5}=E_{1|23,5,4}=-E_{1|32,4,5}
. We also note that

E_{1|23,4,5}=E_{2|31,4,5}
and
E_{2|34,1,5}=E_{1|34,2,5}+E_{1|23,4,5}-E_{1|24,3,5}
. Moreover,
E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}
is permutation symmetric in
2,3,4,5
, but
1\leftrightarrow 2
acts via

E^{\mu}_{2|1,3,4,5}=E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}+[k_{3}^{\mu}E_{1|23,4,5}+(3\leftrightarrow 4,5)]
.

In extracting the kinematic half integrand via (
5.9
), we obtain the following contributions
to
{\cal I}_{5}
and
N_{+|\rho(12345)|-}
from the
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
degeneration of
(
5.20
) and (
5.21
)

\displaystyle e_{1|23,4,5}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\tau\rightarrow i\infty}\frac{E_{1|23,4,5}}{2\pi i}=G_{12}+G_{23}+G_{31}

(5.23)

\displaystyle e^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\tau\rightarrow i\infty}\frac{E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}}{2\pi i}=\ell^{\mu}+\big[k_{2}^{\mu}G_{12}+(2\leftrightarrow 3,4,5)\big]\,,

(5.24)

see (
4.31
) for the nodal Green function
G_{ij}
.
As will be detailed in subsection
5.3
below, each of the 7 terms in the alternative form

{\cal I}_{5}(\ell)=\frac{(e_{1|2,3,4,5})_{\mu}t_{+}^{\mu}(1,2,3,4,5)+\big[e_{1|23,4,5}t_{8}(23,1,4,5)+(2,3|2,3,4,5)\big]}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}\sigma_{4}\sigma_{5}}

(5.25)

of (
4.30
) separately integrates to quadratic propagators order by order in the open-string
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion
of (
3.1
). Similarly, the double copy of (
5.25
) organizes the gravitational one-loop matrix element (
3.2
) into
7\times 7
terms with integrands
e_{\ldots}\bar{e}_{\ldots}
that separately integrate
to quadratic propagators at each order in
\alpha^{\prime}
. This refines the observations
of sections
4.4
and
4.5
that the overall expressions for
A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4,5)
and
M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{5,{\rm eff}}
can be expanded in terms of conventional Feynman integrals with quadratic propagators.

At the level of practicalities, the number of terms conspiring in the recombination of linearized propagators to quadratic ones is reduced by a factor of 49 on average for each bilinear of the
e_{\ldots}
in (
5.25
).
At six points, by the 51 homology invariants contributing to
{\cal K}_{6}

Mafra:2017ioj
;
Mafra:2018qqe
,
the size of typical expressions that recombine to quadratic propagators is reduced by a factor of 2601 on average.
This applies to both the loop integrand in supergravity and the
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-expansion of
M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{6,{\rm eff}}
.

5.2.2
General conjectures

The above five-point observations and a variety of checks at six points
22
22

22

We are
grateful to Sebastian Mizera for testing (
5.26
) for all the six-point instances of
E_{P}

seen in
Mafra:2017ioj
;
Mafra:2018qqe
.
motivate us to formulate the following
general conjecture:
for any homology invariant in the sense of
(
5.19
), the contribution to

A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,\ldots,n)
in (
3.1
) can be
rewritten in terms of quadratic propagators. The same is claimed to
hold for any pair of meromorphic and antimeromorphic
homology invariants contributing to
M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm eff}}
in
(
3.2
). In the field-theory limit
\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
, this
conjecture can be formulated within the framework of ambitwistor
strings:

Conjecture 1:

(on quadratic propagators in field-theory amplitudes)

\displaystyle E_{P}(z_{j},\ell,\tau),\ E_{Q}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)\ \text{homology invariant}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{C}^{n-1}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\textrm{tree}}\,\frac{E_{P}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau\rightarrow i\infty)}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\ldots\sigma_{n}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\gamma\in{\rm cyc}(1,2,\ldots,n)}\!\!\!\!\!\!{\rm PT}(+,\gamma(1,2,\ldots,n),-)

can be recombined to quadratic propagators

(5.26)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{C}^{n-1}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\textrm{tree}}\,\frac{E_{P}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau\rightarrow i\infty)\,E_{Q}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau\rightarrow i\infty)}{|\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\ldots\sigma_{n}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{can be recombined to quadratic propagators}\,,

(5.27)

where the labels
P
and
Q
of the homology invariants
may represent any combination of subscripts and vector
indices such as
P\rightarrow 1|23,4,5
or
P\rightarrow\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\mu\hfill}{1|2,3,4,5\hfill}
in case of (
5.20
) or (
5.21
).

The
\alpha^{\prime}
-corrections to the one-loop matrix elements in
(
3.1
) and (
3.2
) rely on the open- and closed-string
motivated measures

\displaystyle\int_{12\ldots n}\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm open}

\displaystyle=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{0=\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<\ldots<\sigma_{n}<\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathrm{d}\sigma_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}\sigma_{n}\,\prod_{j=1}^{n}|\sigma_{j+}|^{2{\alpha^{\prime}}\ell\cdot k_{j}}\prod_{1\leq i<j}^{n}|\sigma_{ij}|^{2{\alpha^{\prime}}k_{i}\cdot k_{j}}+{\rm cyc}(1,2,\ldots,n)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm closed}

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{\mathbb{C}^{n-1}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{n}\prod_{j=1}^{n}|\sigma_{j+}|^{4{\alpha^{\prime}}\ell\cdot k_{j}}\prod_{1\leq i<j}^{n}|\sigma_{ij}|^{4{\alpha^{\prime}}k_{i}\cdot k_{j}}\,.

(5.28)

In this setting, we additionally claim that, order by order in the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion,

Conjecture 2:

(on quadratic propagators in one-loop matrix elements)

\displaystyle E_{P}(z_{j},\ell,\tau),\ E_{Q}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)\ \text{homology invariant}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\,\int_{12\ldots n}\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\textrm{open}}\,\frac{E_{P}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau\rightarrow i\infty)}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\ldots\sigma_{n}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{can be recombined to quadratic propagators}\,,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\ \ \ \int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{C}^{n-1}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\textrm{closed}}\,\frac{E_{P}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau\rightarrow i\infty)\,\overline{E_{Q}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau\rightarrow i\infty)}}{|\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\ldots\sigma_{n}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{can be recombined to quadratic propagators}\,.

(5.30)

These conditions are sufficient (though not necessary) for the one-loop matrix elements at four
and five points to admit quadratic-propagator representations at all orders in
\alpha^{\prime}
as exemplified in section
4
. At the same time, (
5.26
) to (
5.30
) do not follow from the quadratic-propagator
representations of the overall
n
-point matrix elements with
n>4
since kinematic relations (
4.38
) may
in principle accommodate deviations at the level of the individual homology invariants in (
5.22
).
For both the five-point examples in (
5.20
), (
5.21
) and the higher-point homology
invariants
E_{P}
in
Mafra:2017ioj
;
Mafra:2018pll
;
Mafra:2018qqe
, the integrands
of (
5.29
) and (
5.30
) can be brought into
Parke-Taylor form by the techniques in
He:2017spx
.
Hence, (
5.29
) and (
5.30
) boil down to combinations of disk and sphere integrals
and line up with terms in the one-loop matrix elements (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), respectively.

The BCJ and KLT relations of section
3.2
and their

\alpha^{\prime}
-uplifts are formulated in terms of the linearized
propagators obtained from
m
or the
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-expansion of
Z
and

J
. It would be interesting to reformulate these relations in terms
of quadratic propagators, for instance on the basis of the conjectures
(
5.26
) to (
5.30
). BCJ relations involving quadratic
propagators have been discussed in
Boels:2011tp
;
Boels:2011mn
;
Du:2012mt
;
Primo:2016omk
, whereas quadratic-propagator formulations
of one-loop KLT relations are uncharted territory at the time of
writing.

5.3
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-expansions due to individual homology invariants

We shall now illustrate the conjectures of the previous subsection by spelling
out various examples of
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansions in terms of quadratic propagators.
At four points, the open-string expressions (
4.7
), (
4.12
)
and closed-string expressions (
4.20
), (
4.21
) of this type can be
attributed to a single trivial homology invariant
E_{1|2,3,4}=1
. That is why in most
of our nontrivial examples of (
5.29
) and (
5.30
), the quantities

E_{P},E_{Q}
are specialized to the five-point homology invariants in (
5.20
) and (
5.21
).

5.3.1
Shorthands

It will be convenient to introduce shorthand notations for the contributions of
homology invariants and their bilinears to open- and closed-string one-loop
matrix elements (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), respectively. For open strings,
the single-trace objects
A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,\ldots,n)
are built from
dual pairings of cycles
a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{n}\cong a_{2}a_{3}\ldots a_{n}a_{1}
and a single homology invariant

\displaystyle[a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{n}\,|\,E_{P}\rangle

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\prime n-1}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\,\int_{a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{n}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm open}\,\frac{E_{P}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau\rightarrow i\infty)}{(2\pi i)^{n-4}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\ldots\sigma_{n}}\,,

(5.31)

see (
5.28
) for the definition of the
\alpha^{\prime}
-dependent measures

\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm open},\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm closed}
.
One-loop matrix elements of closed strings in turn are built from sphere integrals
describing the pairing of meromorphic and antimeromorphic homology invariants

\displaystyle\langle E_{Q}\,|\,E_{P}\rangle

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}}{\pi}\bigg)^{n-1}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm closed}\,\frac{E_{P}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau{\rightarrow}i\infty)\,\overline{E_{Q}(\sigma_{j},\ell,\tau{\rightarrow}i\infty)}}{(2\pi)^{2n-8}|\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\ldots\sigma_{n}|^{2}}\,.

(5.32)

By comparison with (
5.29
) and (
5.30
), the conjectures
in the previous section state that the
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-expansions of any such
[a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{n}|E_{P}\rangle

and
\langle E_{Q}|E_{P}\rangle
are expressible in terms of quadratic propagators. By
(
5.9
) and (
5.22
), the five-point
examples below can be assembled to yield the one-loop matrix elements in section
4
via

\displaystyle A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4,5)=[12345\,|\,E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle t_{+\mu}(1,2,3,4,5)

(5.33)

\displaystyle\ \ +[12345\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle t_{8}(23,1,4,5)+[12345\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle t_{8}(24,1,3,5)

\displaystyle\ \ +[12345\,|\,E_{1|25,3,4}\rangle t_{8}(25,1,3,4)+[12345\,|\,E_{1|34,2,5}\rangle t_{8}(34,1,2,5)

\displaystyle\ \ +[12345\,|\,E_{1|35,2,4}\rangle t_{8}(35,1,2,4)+[12345\,|\,E_{1|45,2,3}\rangle t_{8}(45,1,2,3)\,,

as well as (with
\bar{t}_{\pm\nu}\rightarrow\bar{t}_{+\nu}
for type-IIB amplitudes
and
\bar{t}_{\pm\nu}\rightarrow\bar{t}_{-\nu}
for those of type IIA)

\displaystyle M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{5,{\rm eff}}

\displaystyle=\bar{t}_{\pm\nu}(1,2,3,4,5)\langle E^{\nu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\,|\,E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle t_{+\mu}(1,2,3,4,5)

(5.34)

\displaystyle\ \ +\bigg[\bar{t}_{8}(23,1,4,5)\Big(\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle t_{+\mu}(1,2,3,4,5)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle t_{8}(23,1,4,5)+\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle t_{8}(24,1,3,5)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|25,3,4}\rangle t_{8}(25,1,3,4)+\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|34,2,5}\rangle t_{8}(34,1,2,5)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|35,2,4}\rangle t_{8}(35,1,2,4)+\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|45,2,3}\rangle t_{8}(45,1,2,3)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\bar{t}_{\pm\nu}(1,2,3,4,5)\langle E^{\nu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle t_{8}(23,1,4,5)+(2,3|2,3,4,5)\bigg]\,.

At four points, in turn, the one-loop matrix elements (
4.6
) and (
4.18
)
both result from a single pairing of the form (
5.31
) and (
5.32
) with trivial homology invariants
E_{P},E_{Q}\rightarrow 1
,

\displaystyle A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4)

\displaystyle=t_{8}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})\,[1234\,|\,E{=}1\rangle\,,

(5.35)

\displaystyle M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}

\displaystyle=\big|t_{8}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})\big|^{2}\,\langle E{=}1\,|\,E{=}1\rangle\,.

The results in sections
4.1
and
4.2
are then equivalent to the following leading orders

\displaystyle[1234\,|\,E{=}1\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\lim_{k_{\pm}\rightarrow\pm\ell}\sum_{\gamma\in{\rm cyc}(1,2,3,4)}\sum_{\rho\in S_{4}}Z(+,\gamma(1,2,3,4),-|+,\rho(1,2,3,4),-)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{1})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{12})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{123})^{2}}

(5.36)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{1})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{12})^{2}}+{\rm cyc}_{\ell}(1,2,3,4)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\langle E{=}1\,|\,E{=}1\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\lim_{k_{\pm}\rightarrow\pm\ell}\sum_{\gamma,\rho\in S_{4}}J(+,\gamma(1,2,3,4),-|+,\rho(1,2,3,4),-)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\bigg[\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{1})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{12})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{123})^{2}}+{\rm perm}(2,3,4)\bigg]

(5.37)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}^{2}}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{1})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{12})^{2}}+{\rm perm}(1,2,3,4)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})\bigg\}\,,

see (
4.7
) and (
4.20
) for a further subleading order of both.

5.3.2
Five-point open-string examples

With the shorthands
\ell_{12\ldots j}=\ell{+}k_{12\ldots j}
of (
4.36
) for the
momenta in the propagators of the planar
(1,2,3,4,5)
-ordering, the cyclically inequivalent
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-expansions
relevant to (
5.33
) are

\displaystyle[12345\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\frac{-1}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{1}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{1}{s_{23}\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}

(5.38)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{15}}{2\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{34}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{45}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}-\frac{s_{12}+2s_{\ell,2}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\quad-\frac{s_{23}+2s_{13}+2s_{\ell,3}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{45}}{s_{23}\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+\frac{s_{45}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+\frac{s_{15}}{s_{23}\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{15}}{s_{23}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\quad+\frac{s_{34}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\bigg(\frac{1}{s_{12}}+\frac{1}{s_{23}}\bigg)\frac{s_{45}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\bigg(\frac{s_{34}}{s_{12}}-1\bigg)\frac{1}{\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}\bigg]+\mathcal{O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\bigg\}

\displaystyle[12345\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\frac{-1}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{1}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}

(5.39)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{15}}{2\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{23}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{34}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{45}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\quad-\frac{s_{12}+2s_{\ell,2}}{2\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+\frac{s_{45}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+\frac{s_{45}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\quad+\frac{s_{34}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\bigg(\frac{s_{34}}{s_{12}}-1\bigg)\frac{1}{\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\bigg\}

\displaystyle[12345\,|\,E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\frac{\ell^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}k_{1}^{\mu}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}}{s_{15}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}

(5.40)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}\bigg[{-}\bigg(\ell^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}k_{1}^{\mu}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{s_{15}}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{23}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{34}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{45}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\quad-\frac{(\ell^{\mu}+\tfrac{1}{2}k_{1}^{\mu}+k_{2}^{\mu})s_{12}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}s_{\ell,2}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}s_{\ell,1}}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{k_{3}^{\mu}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{k_{4}^{\mu}}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\quad+\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}s_{45}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}s_{45}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}s_{34}}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}s_{34}}{s_{15}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}s_{34}}{s_{15}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\quad-\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}s_{23}}{s_{15}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\bigg(1-\frac{s_{23}}{s_{15}}\bigg)\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+\bigg(\frac{s_{34}}{s_{12}}-1\bigg)\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}}{\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}\bigg]+\mathcal{O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\bigg\}\,.

By inserting into (
5.33
) and using the kinematic identity (
4.38
), we reproduce
the SYM integrand (
4.37
) in the
\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
limit and the
\alpha^{\prime 2}\zeta_{2}F^{4}
-
numerators (
4.41
) from the subleading order.
Note that the expressions in (
5.38
) to (
5.40
) are compatible with the Koba-Nielsen dervatives

\frac{1}{{\alpha^{\prime}}}\partial_{z_{2}}\log{\cal J}_{5}(\ell,\tau)=(k_{2})_{\mu}E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}+k_{2}\cdot k_{3}E_{1|23,4,5}+k_{2}\cdot k_{4}E_{1|24,3,5}+k_{2}\cdot k_{5}E_{1|25,3,4}

(5.41)

following from (
5.2
) which drop out from one-loop string amplitudes.
At the level of one-loop matrix elements and their building blocks
(
5.31
) and (
5.32
), the total derivatives (
5.41
)
clearly lead to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(k_{2})_{\mu}[a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{5}\,|\,E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle+k_{2}\cdot k_{3}\,[a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{5}\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle

(5.42)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +k_{2}\cdot k_{4}\,[a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{5}\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle+k_{2}\cdot k_{5}\,[a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{5}\,|\,E_{1|25,3,4}\rangle

and analogous integration-by-parts identities with
\langle E_{Q}|
in the place of
[a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{5}|
.

5.3.3
Five-point closed-string examples

The gravitational five-point one-loop matrix element (
5.34
) involves five
pairings (
5.32
) of homology invariants that are inequivalent under permutations
of
2,3,4,5
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\bullet\ \langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle&\bullet\ \langle E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle\\
\bullet\ \langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle&\bullet\ \langle E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\,|\,E^{\nu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle\\
\bullet\ \langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|45,2,3}\rangle\end{array}

Already the leading orders of their
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion are considerably longer than
their open-string counterparts and are therefore relegated to ancillary files. For instance,
the field-theory limit

\displaystyle\langle E_{1|23,4,5}\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\sum_{\rho\in S_{3}}\frac{1}{s_{12}\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{12\rho(3)}^{2}\ell_{12\rho(34)}^{2}}

(5.43)

\displaystyle\ \ +\sum_{\rho\in S_{4}}\frac{{\rm sgn}_{23}^{\rho}{\rm sgn}_{24}^{\rho}}{4\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{1\rho(2)}^{2}\ell_{1\rho(23)}^{2}\ell_{1\rho(234)}}+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\bigg\}

involves 6 boxes and 24 pentagons. The signs of the pentagons are governed by

{\rm sgn}_{ij}^{\rho}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}+1&:\ \textrm{$i$ is on the right of $j$ in $\rho(2,3,4,5)$}\\
-1&:\ \textrm{$i$ is on the left of $j$ in $\rho(2,3,4,5)$}\\
\end{array}\right.

(5.44)

introduced by the interplay of the nodal Green function (
4.31
)
with Parke-Taylor factors, see section 3 of
He:2017spx
for details.
Note that the integration-by-parts identity (
5.42
) of the open string straightforwardly
carries over to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(k_{2})_{\mu}\,\langle E_{P}\,|\,E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle+k_{2}\cdot k_{3}\,\langle E_{P}\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle

(5.45)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +k_{2}\cdot k_{4}\,\langle E_{P}\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle+k_{2}\cdot k_{5}\,\langle E_{P}\,|\,E_{1|25,3,4}\rangle\,.

5.4
All-multiplicity formulae

While a detailed study of one-loop matrix elements at
n\geq 6
points
is relegated to the future, we shall now present all-multiplicity
conjectures for scalar integrals over symmetrized Parke-Taylor
factors. As an
\alpha^{\prime}
-uplift of the
n
-gon formulae in the ambitwistor
setup
Geyer:2015bja
;
He:2015yua
;
Geyer:2015jch
(with

\ell_{12\ldots j}=\ell+k_{12\ldots j}
),

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm tree}}{\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}\ldots\sigma_{n}}\sum_{\gamma\in{\rm cyc}(1,2,\ldots,n)}{\rm PT}(+,\gamma(1,2,\ldots,n),-)=\int\frac{(-1)^{n}\,\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}\ell^{2}_{1}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm tree}}{|\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}\ldots\sigma_{n}|^{2}}=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}\ell^{2}_{1}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}+{\rm perm}(2,3,\ldots,n)\,,

(5.46)

we shall investigate the first string corrections to the symmetrized integrals

\displaystyle Z^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm symm}(1,2,\ldots,n)

\displaystyle=[12\ldots n\,|\,(2\pi i)^{n-4}\rangle=\alpha^{\prime n-1}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int_{12\ldots n}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm open}}{\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}\ldots\sigma_{n}}

(5.47)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\lim_{k_{\pm}\rightarrow\pm\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in{\rm cyc}(1,2,\ldots,n)\\
\rho\in S_{n}\end{subarray}}Z(+,\gamma(1,2,\ldots,n),-|+,\rho(1,2,\ldots,n),-)\,,

\displaystyle J^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm symm}}

\displaystyle=\langle(2\pi i)^{n-4}\,|\,(2\pi i)^{n-4}\rangle=\bigg(\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\pi}\bigg)^{n-1}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm closed}}{|\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}\ldots\sigma_{n}|^{2}}

(5.48)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\ell^{2}}\lim_{k_{\pm}\rightarrow\pm\ell}\sum_{\gamma,\rho\in S_{n}}J(+,\gamma(1,2,\ldots,n),-|+,\rho(1,2,\ldots,n),-)\,,

see (
5.28
) for the definition of the measures
\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm open}
and

\mathrm{d}\mu_{n+2}^{\rm closed}
. These all-multiplicity families of integrals are engineered to
reproduce the
n
-gon integrals (
5.46
) in their field-theory limits. The four- and five-point
instances of the symmetrized open-string integrals,

\displaystyle Z^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm symm}(1,2,3,4)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\frac{1}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}-\alpha^{\prime 2}\zeta_{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+{\rm cyc}_{\ell}(1,2,3,4)\bigg]

(5.49)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt+\frac{\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}}{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{12}(s_{1,\ell}{-}s_{2,\ell})}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+\frac{s_{12}{-}2s_{13}{+}s_{23}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+{\rm cyc}_{\ell}(1,2,3,4)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})\bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle Z^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm symm}(1,2,3,4,5)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\frac{1}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}-\alpha^{\prime 2}\zeta_{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+{\rm cyc}(1,2,3,4,5)\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt+\frac{\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}}{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{12}(s_{1,\ell}{-}s_{2,\ell})}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+\frac{s_{12}{-}2s_{13}{+}s_{23}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+{\rm cyc}_{\ell}(1,2,3,4,5)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})\bigg\}\,,

augment the
n
-gon integral in the prescribed color ordering by

•

a cyclic orbit of
(n{-}1)
gons at
the order of the
\alpha^{\prime 2}\zeta_{2}F^{4}
and
\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}(D^{2}F^{4}+F^{5})
interactions

•

by additional one-mass
(n{-}2)
gons at
the
\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}(D^{2}F^{4}+F^{5})
-order.

The notation
+{\rm cyc}_{\ell}(1,2,\ldots,n)
instructs to shift loop momenta in the
cyclic images of the numerators
\sim s_{1,\ell}{-}s_{2,\ell}
as explained
below (
4.7
).

The same patterns of
(n{-}1)
and
(n{-}2)
gons are verified to arise
at six points which motivates the
n
-point conjecture,

\displaystyle Z^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm symm}(1,2,\ldots,n)=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\frac{1}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}-\bigg[\frac{\alpha^{\prime 2}\zeta_{2}s_{12}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}+{\rm cyc}_{\ell}(1,2,\ldots,n)\bigg]

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}}{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{12}(s_{1,\ell}{-}s_{2,\ell})}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}+\frac{s_{12}{-}2s_{13}{+}s_{23}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}+{\rm cyc}_{\ell}(1,2,\ldots,n)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})\bigg\}\,.

(5.50)

Similarly, the closed-string results at four and five points,

\displaystyle J^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm symm}}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\bigg[\frac{1}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+{\rm perm}(2,3,4)\bigg]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}^{2}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}}+{\rm perm}(1,2,3,4)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})\bigg\}\,,

(5.51)

\displaystyle J^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{5,{\rm symm}}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\bigg[\frac{1}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+{\rm perm}(2,3,4,5)\bigg]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}\bigg[\frac{s^{2}_{12}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ell_{123}^{2}\ell_{1234}^{2}}+{\rm perm}(1,2,3,4,5)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})\bigg\}\,,

involve a characteristic permutation sum over scalar
(n{-}1)
-gons at the order of the
\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}R^{4}
interaction.
We propose the
n
-point generalization,

\displaystyle J^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm symm}}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\,\bigg\{\bigg[\frac{1}{\ell^{2}\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}+{\rm perm}(2,3,\ldots,n)\bigg]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}\bigg[\frac{s_{12}^{2}}{\ell^{2}\ell_{12}^{2}\ldots\ell_{12\ldots n-1}^{2}}+{\rm perm}(1,2,\ldots,n)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{5})\bigg\}\,,

(5.52)

which has also been tested at six points. In fact, these expressions for
J^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm symm}}

follow from our conjecture (
5.50
) for
Z^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm symm}(1,2,\ldots,n)
since
the definitions (
5.47
) and (
5.48
) together with the tree-level relation
J={\rm sv}\,Z
imply that

J^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm symm}}={\rm sv}\,Z^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm symm}}(1,2,\ldots,n)+{\rm perm}(2,3,\ldots,n)\,.

(5.53)

The permutation sum removes the antisymmetric part
s_{1,\ell}{-}s_{2,\ell}
from the
(n{-}1)
-gon
numerators of
{\rm sv}\,Z
and furthermore cancels the
(n{-}2)
-gons since
s_{12}{-}2s_{13}{+}s_{23}+{\rm perm}(1,2,3)=0
.

6
UV divergences versus degenerations of one-loop string amplitudes

In this section, we support our proposal for one-loop matrix elements
with insertions of
D^{2k}F^{n}
and
D^{2k}R^{n}
by comparing their
UV divergences with expectations from string
theory. More specifically, the behaviour of genus-one string
integrands at the boundary
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
of moduli space
encodes the UV divergences of one-loop matrix elements computed from
low-energy effective actions
Metsaev:1987ju
;
Green:2010sp
;
Pioline:2018pso
. We
will determine the UV divergences of the one-loop matrix elements
presented in earlier sections and verify their compatibility with
degenerate genus-one integrals of the corresponding string amplitude.

6.1
Basics of degenerating one-loop closed-string amplitudes

In relating one-loop matrix elements to string amplitudes in section

5.1
and appendix
B
, we have first performed
the integration over
\tau
and then
z_{j}
within a certain
approximation while leaving the loop momentum
\ell
unintegrated. The
discussion of the present section will follow the more conventional
procedure
Polchinski:1998rq
;
Green:1987mn
to first integrate
over
\ell
which manifests modular invariance of closed-string genus-one amplitudes.

Based on properties of modular graph forms
DHoker:2015wxz
;
DHoker:2016mwo
, we will investigate the integrals over
\ell

followed by
z_{j}
in the expansion around the cusp

\tau\rightarrow i\infty
. The dominant behaviour of these integrals
at the cusp is governed by Laurent polynomials in
\pi\Im\tau
with

\mathbb{Q}
-linear combinations of MZVs (conjecturally single-valued
MZVs
Zerbini:2015rss
;
DHoker:2015wxz
) in their coefficients.
Each term is in one-to-one correspondence with the UV divergences of
effective field theories in various spacetime dimensions

Green:2010sp
;
Pioline:2018pso
.

6.1.1
Modular graph forms and their degeneration

After performing the Gaussian loop integral, the closed-string
four-point amplitude (
5.1
) is proportional to
23
23

23

By different
conventions for the definition (
2.11
) of
s_{ij}
in this work, the expressions in (
6.1
) and
(
6.4
) can be obtained from results of
DHoker:2015gmr
;
DHoker:2015wxz

by setting
s_{ij}\rightarrow-{\alpha^{\prime}}s_{ij}
in the references.

M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm closed}}\sim|t_{8}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})|^{2}\int_{\mathfrak{F}}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\tau}{(\Im\tau)^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\tau}^{3}}\bigg(\prod_{j=2}^{4}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{j}}{\Im\tau}\bigg)\,\exp\bigg({-}{\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{1\leq i<j}^{4}s_{ij}{\cal G}(z_{ij},\tau)\bigg)

(6.1)

with modular invariant measures
\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\tau}{(\Im\tau)^{2}}
and

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{j}}{\Im\tau}
. The modular invariant closed-string Green function
is given in terms of the
\theta_{1}
function (
5.3
), the Dedekind eta
function
\eta(\tau)=q^{1/24}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}

(1-q^{n})

and the comoving coordinates
u,v\in\mathbb{R}
for
z=u\tau{+}v
:

{\cal G}(z,\tau)=-\log\bigg|\frac{\theta_{1}(z,\tau)}{\eta(\tau)}\bigg|^{2}+\frac{2\pi(\Im z)^{2}}{\Im\tau}=\frac{\Im\tau}{\pi}\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}\atop{(m,n)\neq(0,0)}}\frac{e^{2\pi i(nu-mv)}}{|m\tau+n|^{2}}\,.

(6.2)

The key information on the low-energy effective action can be obtained
by Taylor-expanding the integrand of (
6.1
) in
{\alpha^{\prime}}
: upon
integration over
z_{j}
, this leads to a power series in
{\alpha^{\prime}}s_{ij}

whose coefficients are the modular invariant functions of
\tau
that
result from integrating monomials in Green functions
{\cal G}(z_{ij},\tau)

over their first arguments. These
z_{j}
-integrations can be
systematically performed using the lattice-sum representation in
(
6.2
) and the resulting nested lattice sums are known as
modular graph forms
DHoker:2015wxz
;
DHoker:2016mwo
. The study
of modular graph forms became a vibrant research topic at the
interface of string theory, algebraic geometry and number theory, see
e.g.
Brown:2017qwo
;
Brown:2017qwo2
for a conjectural
mathematical framework and the PhD thesis
Gerken:review
for a
recent overview from a physics perspective.

In an expansion of modular graph forms around the cusp

\tau\rightarrow i\infty
, the dominant contributions are Laurent
polynomials in

y=\pi\Im\tau

(6.3)

up to terms that are exponentially suppressed with
q
or
\bar{q}
,
i.e.
e^{-2y}
in both cases. For the leading orders of the

\tau
-integrand in (
6.1
), we have the degeneration

\displaystyle m^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm closed}}(\tau)

\displaystyle=(4{\alpha^{\prime}})^{5}(\Im\tau)^{2}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\tau}^{3}}\prod_{j=2}^{4}\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{j}\int\mathrm{d}^{10}\ell\,\big|{\cal J}_{4}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)\big|^{2}\,\Big|_{q^{0}\bar{q}^{0}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\tau}^{3}}\bigg(\prod_{j=2}^{4}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{j}}{\Im\tau}\bigg)\,\exp\bigg({-}{\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{1\leq i<j}^{4}s_{ij}{\cal G}(z_{ij},\tau)\bigg)\,\Big|_{q^{0}\bar{q}^{0}}

\displaystyle=1+2{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}(s_{12}^{2}{-}s_{13}s_{23})\bigg(\frac{y^{2}}{45}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{y}\bigg)-{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}s_{12}s_{13}s_{23}\bigg(\frac{2y^{3}}{189}+\zeta_{3}+\frac{15\zeta_{5}}{4y^{2}}\bigg)

(6.4)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4}(s_{12}^{2}{-}s_{13}s_{23})^{2}\bigg(\frac{16y^{4}}{14175}+\frac{8y\zeta_{3}}{45}+\frac{5\zeta_{5}}{3y}+\frac{\zeta_{3}^{2}}{y^{2}}+\frac{\zeta_{7}}{y^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5}s_{12}s_{13}s_{23}(s_{13}s_{23}{-}s_{12}^{2})\bigg(\frac{8y^{5}}{22275}{+}\frac{2y^{2}\zeta_{3}}{21}{+}\frac{58\zeta_{5}}{45}{+}\frac{2\zeta_{3}^{2}}{y}{+}\frac{49\zeta_{7}}{8y^{2}}{+}\frac{\zeta_{3}\zeta_{5}}{2y^{3}}{+}\frac{63\zeta_{9}}{32y^{4}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})\,,

see for instance
Zerbini:2015rss
;
DHoker:2019xef
;
Zagier:2019eus
;
Gerken:2020yii
;
Vanhove:2020qtt
for the systematics
at higher order. The notation
|_{q^{0}\bar{q}^{0}}
in the first two lines instructs to only keep the
m=n=0

terms in the expansion
\sim(\Im\tau)^{a}q^{m}\bar{q}^{n}
around the cusp. As will be detailed in

sections

6.5
and
C
below, each term in (
6.4
) accurately
matches the UV divergences of the closed-string one-loop matrix elements
in section
4.2
, where the number of spacetime dimensions
correlates with the power of
y
.

6.1.2
Analytic versus non-analytic sector of one-loop amplitudes

The step of performing the
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-expansion of (
6.1
) at the
level of the
\tau
-integrand is oblivious to the intricate branch
cuts of the closed-string one-loop amplitudes

DHoker:1994gnm
. Among other things, the power series in
(
6.4
) does not take the discontinuities of the form

\log(\alpha^{\prime}s_{ij})
into account which can for instance be
determined from the Feynman integrals in section
4
(see
section
7
for four-point examples). Instead,
(
6.4
) captures the rational functions in
s_{ij}
obtained as
the residues of the UV divergences, and the
{\cal O}(e^{-2y})
terms
which are accurately known to the order of
({\alpha^{\prime}}s_{ij})^{6}
can be
used to infer the one-loop effective action. The interplay between the
power-series behaved terms (the “analytic sector”) of closed-string
one-loop amplitudes and the branch cuts (the “non-analytic sector”)
has for instance been discussed in
Green:2008uj
;
DHoker:2015gmr
;
DHoker:2019blr
. In section
7
, we will spell out the
discontinuities of one-loop four-point matrix elements which determine
the non-analytic sector of closed- and open-string amplitudes.

6.1.3
Degenerate modular graph forms at five points

The low-energy expansion of the closed-string five-point function can
again be organized into modular graph forms
Green:2013bza
after
integrating
|{\cal K}_{5}{\cal J}_{5}|^{2}
over the loop momenta. Since the chiral
five-point correlator
{\cal K}_{5}
in (
5.10
) is a sum of seven homology
invariants
E_{P}
, it will be convenient to introduce a shorthand for
the five-point analogues of the four-point building block in
(
6.4
),

\langle\!\langle E_{Q}|E_{P}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}=2^{10}({\alpha^{\prime}})^{6}(\Im\tau)^{2}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\tau}^{4}}\prod_{j=2}^{5}\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{j}\int\mathrm{d}^{10}\ell\,E_{P}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)\,\overline{E}_{Q}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)\,\big|{\cal J}_{5}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)\big|^{2}\,\Big|_{q^{0}\bar{q}^{0}}\,.

(6.5)

Similar to section
5.3.3
, the
7\times 7
bilinears

\overline{E}_{Q}E_{P}
arising from
|{\cal K}_{5}|^{2}
can be organized
into five permutation inequivalent cases

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle E_{1|23,4,5}|E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{s_{12}}-\frac{1}{s_{13}}-\frac{1}{s_{23}}+2{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg(\frac{y^{2}}{45}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{y}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{s_{34}s_{35}}{s_{12}}-\frac{s_{45}^{2}}{s_{12}}+\frac{s_{24}s_{25}}{s_{13}}-\frac{s_{45}^{2}}{s_{13}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\frac{s_{14}s_{15}}{s_{23}}-\frac{s_{45}^{2}}{s_{23}}+s_{45}\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle E_{1|23,4,5}|E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{s_{12}}+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg(\frac{y^{2}}{45}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{y}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{2(s_{35}s_{45}-s_{34}^{2})}{s_{12}}-s_{12}+5s_{34}\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle E_{1|23,4,5}|E_{1|45,2,3}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle={\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg(\frac{y^{2}}{45}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{y}\bigg)(s_{24}+s_{35}-s_{25}-s_{34})+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\,,

(6.6)

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle E_{1|23,4,5}|E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}}{s_{12}}+\frac{k_{3}^{\mu}}{s_{13}}+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg(\frac{y^{2}}{45}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{y}\bigg)\bigg(k_{3}^{\mu}\frac{(s_{24}^{2}{+}s_{25}^{2}{+}s_{45}^{2})}{s_{13}}-k_{2}^{\mu}\frac{(s_{34}^{2}{+}s_{35}^{2}{+}s_{45}^{2})}{s_{12}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +(k_{3}^{\mu}-k_{2}^{\mu})s_{45}+k_{4}^{\mu}(s_{24}-s_{34})+k_{5}^{\mu}(s_{25}-s_{35})\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle E^{\nu}_{1|2,3,4,5}|E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle={-}\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}k_{2}^{\nu}}{s_{12}}-\frac{k_{3}^{\mu}k_{3}^{\nu}}{s_{13}}-\frac{k_{4}^{\mu}k_{4}^{\nu}}{s_{14}}-\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}k_{5}^{\nu}}{s_{15}}

\displaystyle\hskip-12.91663pt+2{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg(\frac{y^{2}}{45}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{y}\bigg)\bigg(k_{2}^{\mu}k_{2}^{\nu}\bigg[\frac{(s_{34}s_{35}{+}s_{34}s_{45}{+}s_{35}s_{45})}{s_{12}}-s_{12}\bigg]+(2\leftrightarrow 3,4,5)\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip-12.91663pt+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\bigg(\frac{y^{2}}{45}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{y}\bigg)\bigg[(k_{2}^{\mu}k_{3}^{\nu}+k_{2}^{\nu}k_{3}^{\mu})s_{23}+(2,3|2,3,4,5)\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{3})\,.

While the loop integrals in (
6.4
) and (
6.5
) have
been performed in
D=10
dimensions, we will later on be interested in
UV divergences in arbitrary dimensions. The
D
-dimensional versions
of the closed-string loop integrals follow from inserting a
factor of
(2\sqrt{{\alpha^{\prime}}\Im\tau})^{10-D}
due to the modified
Gaussian integral and a series in non-integer powers of
q,\bar{q}
to accommodate
Kaluza-Klein and winding modes of toroidal compactifications
Green:1982sw
.
These
q,\bar{q}
series do not contribute to the
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
limit relevant for
our one-loop matrix elements, see section
5.1.3
.

6.2
Basics of degenerating one-loop open-string amplitudes

After integrating out the loop momentum in one-loop open-string
amplitudes (
5.4
), the integrand w.r.t. the modular
parameter
\tau
admits similar expansions around the cusp as in the
closed-string case: when focusing on the analytic sector in

\alpha^{\prime}s_{ij}

systematically performed in terms of elliptic multiple zeta values

;

. The dominant contributions as

\tau\rightarrow i\infty
are Laurent polynomials in

T=\pi\tau

(6.7)

instead of the
y=\pi\Im\tau
in the closed-string degenerations,
with
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combinations of MZVs in its coefficients. In
the four-point amplitude (

), we have

Broedel:2018izr
;
Gerken:2018jrq
24
24

24

By the different
conventions for the definition of

, the following

;

by setting

\displaystyle a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}(1,2,3,4;\tau)=(-2i{\alpha^{\prime}})^{5}\tau^{2}\int_{\mathfrak{C}_{\tau}(1,2,3,4)}\prod_{j=2}^{4}\mathrm{d}z_{j}\int\mathrm{d}^{10}\ell\,|{\cal J}_{4}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)|\,\Big|_{q^{0}}

\displaystyle\ \ =\int_{0<u_{2}<u_{3}<u_{4}<1}\bigg(\prod_{j=2}^{4}\mathrm{d}u_{j}\bigg)\exp\bigg({\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{1\leq i<j}^{4}s_{ij}\big[\log\theta_{1}(|u_{ij}|\tau,\tau)+i\pi u_{ij}^{2}\tau\big]\bigg)\,\Big|_{q^{0}}

\displaystyle\ \ =\frac{1}{6}+\alpha^{\prime}s_{13}\bigg({-}\frac{iT}{60}+\frac{i\zeta_{2}}{2T}+\frac{3\zeta_{3}}{2T^{2}}-\frac{3i\zeta_{4}}{2T^{3}}\bigg)+\alpha^{\prime 2}(s_{12}^{2}-s_{13}s_{23})\bigg({-}\frac{T^{2}}{540}+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{9}+\frac{i\zeta_{3}}{3T}+\frac{\zeta_{4}}{2T^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\alpha^{\prime 2}(s_{12}^{2}+4s_{12}s_{23}+s_{23}^{2})\bigg(\frac{T^{2}}{3780}-\frac{\zeta_{2}}{36}+\frac{\zeta_{4}}{4T^{2}}-\frac{i\zeta_{5}}{T^{3}}-\frac{5\zeta_{6}}{4T^{4}}\bigg)

(6.8)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\alpha^{\prime 3}s_{12}s_{23}s_{13}\bigg({-}\frac{iT^{3}}{4536}+\frac{iT\zeta_{2}}{45}-\frac{\zeta_{3}}{12}-\frac{35i\zeta_{4}}{24T}-\frac{2\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}}{T^{2}}+\frac{5\zeta_{5}}{4T^{2}}+\frac{17i\zeta_{6}}{12T^{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\alpha^{\prime 3}s_{13}(s_{12}^{2}-s_{13}s_{23})\bigg(\frac{iT^{3}}{7560}-\frac{iT\zeta_{2}}{90}+\frac{\zeta_{3}}{20}+\frac{3i\zeta_{4}}{4T}+\frac{5\zeta_{5}}{2T^{2}}-\frac{\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}}{2T^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\frac{29i\zeta_{6}}{12T^{3}}-\frac{3\zeta_{7}}{T^{4}}-\frac{3\zeta_{3}\zeta_{4}}{2T^{4}}+\frac{21i\zeta_{8}}{4T^{5}}\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})\,,

where
z_{j}=u_{j}\tau
and
|_{q^{0}}
instructs to only keep the

terms
in the expansion
\sim\tau^{a}q^{n}
around the cusp. The methods of

;

;

can also be used to infer the

at any order in

in terms of B-cycle elliptic multiple zeta values

;

;

have only displayed the dominant terms in the expansion of the

\tau

6.2.1
Open- versus closed-string Laurent polynomials

A direct comparison between (
6.8
) and the closed-string
counterpart (
6.4
) can be made upon symmetrization over the
four-point color orderings. Mandelstam identities imply that for
instance the entire first
{\alpha^{\prime}}
-order as well as the Laurent
coefficients
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{5}
and
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{7}
drop out from
(
6.8
) upon symmetrization,

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in S_{3}}

\displaystyle a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm open}}(1,\gamma(2,3,4);\tau)=1+\alpha^{\prime 2}(s_{12}^{2}-s_{13}s_{23})\bigg({-}\frac{T^{2}}{90}+\frac{2\zeta_{2}}{3}+\frac{2i\zeta_{3}}{T}+\frac{3\zeta_{4}}{T^{2}}\bigg)

(6.9)

\displaystyle\ +\alpha^{\prime 3}s_{12}s_{23}s_{13}\bigg({-}\frac{iT^{3}}{756}+\frac{2iT\zeta_{2}}{15}-\frac{\zeta_{3}}{2}-\frac{35i\zeta_{4}}{4T}-\frac{12\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}}{T^{2}}+\frac{15\zeta_{5}}{2T^{2}}+\frac{17i\zeta_{6}}{2T^{3}}\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})\,.

As firstly pointed out in
Broedel:2018izr
, these symmetrized
open-string building blocks can be related to the closed-string
degerations (
6.4
) via

m^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm closed}}(\tau)={\rm sv}\sum_{\gamma\in S_{3}}a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm open}}(1,\gamma(2,3,4);\tau)

(6.10)

if the action of the single-valued map (
2.27
) is extended to

{\rm sv}\,T=2iy\,.

(6.11)

The intimate relation between degenerate elliptic multiple zeta values
and modular graph forms has been proven for the two-point analogue of
(
6.10
)
Zagier:2019eus
. Moreover, a conjectural
generalization of (
6.10
) to individual
n
-point open-string
integration cycles can be found in
Gerken:2020xfv
.
As we will see in section
6.5
, the relation (
6.10
)
between closed- and open-string Laurent polynomials can be understood
from the UV divergences in the relation (
4.19
) between one-loop
matrix elements.

6.2.2
Degenerate elliptic multiple zeta values at five points

At five points, we introduce a shorthand similar to (
6.5
) for the contributions
of individual homology invariants to the
\tau
integrand of (
5.4
)

[\![a_{1}\ldots a_{5}\,|\,E_{P}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}=-32i({\alpha^{\prime}})^{6}\tau^{2}\int_{\mathfrak{C}_{\tau}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{5})}\prod_{j=2}^{5}\mathrm{d}z_{j}\int\mathrm{d}^{10}\ell\,E_{P}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)\,|{\cal J}_{5}(z_{j},\ell,\tau)|\,\Big|_{q^{0}}\,.

(6.12)

For a given integration ordering
a_{1}a_{2}\ldots a_{5}\rightarrow 12345
(see (
5.5
)
for the definition of
\mathfrak{C}_{\tau}
), there are three cyclically inequivalent
cases to consider,

\displaystyle[\![12345\,|\,E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6s_{12}}+\frac{1}{6s_{23}}+{\alpha^{\prime}}\bigg(3-\frac{2s_{14}}{s_{23}}-\frac{2s_{35}}{s_{12}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\bigg({-}\frac{iT}{120}{+}\frac{i\zeta_{2}}{4T}{+}\frac{3\zeta_{3}}{4T^{2}}{-}\frac{3i\zeta_{4}}{4T^{3}}\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{2})\,,

\displaystyle[\![12345\,|\,E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6s_{12}}+{\alpha^{\prime}}\bigg(1+\frac{2s_{35}}{s_{12}}\bigg)\bigg({-}\frac{iT}{120}{+}\frac{i\zeta_{2}}{4T}{+}\frac{3\zeta_{3}}{4T^{2}}{-}\frac{3i\zeta_{4}}{4T^{3}}\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{2})\,,\!\!\!

(6.13)

\displaystyle[\![12345\,|\,E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}}{6s_{12}}-\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}}{6s_{15}}+{\alpha^{\prime}}\bigg(k_{24}^{\mu}-k_{35}^{\mu}-\frac{2s_{35}}{s_{12}}k_{2}^{\mu}+\frac{2s_{24}}{s_{15}}k_{5}^{\mu}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\bigg({-}\frac{iT}{120}{+}\frac{i\zeta_{2}}{4T}{+}\frac{3\zeta_{3}}{4T^{2}}{-}\frac{3i\zeta_{4}}{4T^{3}}\bigg)+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{2})\,.

We have again displayed the ten-dimensional loop integrals in (
6.8
) and (
6.12
),
and the expressions in different dimensions follow by inserting an extra factor of
\sqrt{-2i{\alpha^{\prime}}\tau}^{10-D}

and a series in
q
to take the Kaluza-Klein modes from a toroidal compactification into account
Green:1982sw
.
Again, the
q
-series of the Kaluza-Klein modes do not contribute to the
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
limit
relevant for the one-loop matrix elements under investigation, see section
5.1.3
.

6.3
String Laurent polynomials, Schwinger parameters and UV divergences

In the field-theory limit of one-loop string amplitudes, the limit

\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
is performed while degenerating the torus or
cylinder worldsheet to a worldline via
\Im\tau\rightarrow\infty

Green:1982sw
;
Bern:1990cu
;
Bern:1990ux
;
Bern:1991an
;
Bern:1991aq
;
Bern:1993wt
;
Dunbar:1994bn
;
Schubert:2001he
;
Tourkine:2013rda
;
Mafra:2014gja
. In this process, Feynman integrals
depending on momenta
K_{j}
with possibly non-vanishing
K_{j}^{2}
are retained in their
Schwinger parametrization

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\,\frac{(T_{0}+T_{1}^{\mu}\ell_{\mu}+T_{2}^{\mu\nu}\ell_{\mu}\ell_{\nu}+\ldots)}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}K_{1})^{2}(\ell{+}K_{12})^{2}\ldots(\ell{+}K_{12\ldots n-1})^{2}}=\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,t^{n-D/2}

\displaystyle\ \ \times\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{0<u_{2}<u_{3}<\ldots<u_{n}<1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathrm{d}u_{2}\,\mathrm{d}u_{3}\ldots\mathrm{d}u_{n}\,\exp\bigg[{-}t\!\sum_{1\leq i<j}^{n}\!K_{i}\cdot K_{j}\big(u_{ij}^{2}-|u_{ij}|\big)\bigg]

(6.14)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\bigg(T_{0}+T_{1}^{\mu}L_{\mu}+T_{2}^{\mu\nu}\bigg[L_{\mu}L_{\nu}+\frac{\eta_{\mu\nu}}{2t}\bigg]+\ldots\bigg)\,,

where the worldline proper times
u_{j}
are the first comoving coordinate
of the worldsheet punctures
z_{j}=u_{j}\tau{+}v_{j}
with
u_{j},v_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
.
As will be detailed below, the worldline length
t
is identified with
2\pi\alpha^{\prime}\Im\tau
for
open strings and
4\pi\alpha^{\prime}\Im\tau
for closed strings and remains a dynamical integration
variable in the simultaneous limit
\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
and
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
.
Moreover, (
6.14
) exemplifies
the worldline treatment of loop momenta in the numerators, where

T_{0},T_{1}^{\mu},T_{2}^{\mu\nu}
are arbitrary
\ell
-independent tensors. The quantity

L^{\mu}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}K_{j}^{\mu}u_{j}

(6.15)

stems from the shift of loop momentum to complete the square in the Gaussian integral
over
\ell
, and higher powers
\ell^{N\geq 2}
involve additional
Wick contractions
\ell_{\mu}\ell_{\nu}\rightarrow\frac{\eta_{\mu\nu}}{2t}
.
Finally, the exponential in the second line of
(
6.14
) is the worldline counterpart of the string-theory
Koba-Nielsen factors in (
6.1
) and (
6.8
) with
worldline Green function
u_{ij}^{2}-|u_{ij}|
. We emphasize that
(
6.14
) also applies to any number of massive corners, i.e. momenta in

K_{12\ldots p}=K_{1}{+}K_{2}{+}\ldots{+}K_{p}
subject to
K_{j}^{2}\neq 0
.

In the Schwinger parametrization (
6.14
), UV divergences stem
from the integration region
t\rightarrow 0
of small worldline
lengths, reflecting their origin from short-distance effects (see, for instance,

Smirnov:2012gma
). Thus to study these UV divergences we first Taylor-expand the right-hand side of
eq.

6.14
as

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\,\frac{(T_{0}+T_{1}^{\mu}\ell_{\mu}+T_{2}^{\mu\nu}\ell_{\mu}\ell_{\nu}+\ldots)}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}K_{1})^{2}(\ell{+}K_{12})^{2}\ldots(\ell{+}K_{12\ldots n-1})^{2}}\Bigg|_{\rm UV}=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\sum_{M=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n+M-1-D/2}}{M!}

(6.16)

\displaystyle\ \ \times\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{0<u_{2}<u_{3}<\ldots<u_{n}<1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathrm{d}u_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}u_{n}\,\Bigg[\!\sum_{1\leq i<j}^{n}\!\!K_{i}{\cdot}K_{j}\big(u_{ji}{-}u_{ji}^{2}\big)\Bigg]^{M}\!\bigg(T_{0}+T_{1}^{\mu}L_{\mu}+T_{2}^{\mu\nu}\bigg[L_{\mu}L_{\nu}+\frac{\eta_{\mu\nu}}{2t}\bigg]+\ldots\bigg)\,\,,

where the integration over the simplex
0{=}u_{1}{<}u_{2}{<}u_{3}{<}\ldots{<}u_{n}{<}1
is straightforward for any
M
and
n
, leading to a Laurent series in
t
as the integrand. The
series expansion of the exponential in (
6.14
) generically does not commute with the integration over
t
, and we employ the notation
\int\ldots\big|_{{\rm UV}}
on the left-hand side of (
6.16
) to indicate that the
right-hand side does not provide an exact expression for the original Feynman integral.
Still, the right-hand side of (
6.16
) accurately reproduces
the UV divergences in various spacetime dimensions while the series expansion in
K_{i}\cdot K_{j}
does not
capture the discontinuities such as the
\log(K_{i}\cdot K_{j})
in the
\epsilon
-expansions of appendix
C.4
. Moreover, we have introduced an infrared cutoff
t_{\rm max}
into the integration domain for
t
to decouple infrared divergences from the poles in the
dimensional-regularization parameter
\epsilon
: if we view the Feynman integrals as analytic functions
in
D=D_{\rm crit}{-}2\epsilon
, then the UV divergences
of (
6.16
) in
D_{\rm crit}=2N
are the residues at the
\frac{1}{\epsilon}
pole of

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{N-1-D/2}=\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})\,,

(6.17)

where the dependence on the cutoff
0<t_{\rm max}<\infty
is relegated to the regular terms

\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})
. Upon comparison with (
6.16
), its
M^{\rm th}

order in
K_{i}\cdot K_{j}
captures the UV divergence of a scalar
n
-gon in the critical dimension
2n{+}2M
.
For tensor integrals, the loop momenta in the numerator lower the critical dimension through the
Wick contractions
\ell_{\mu}\ell_{\nu}\rightarrow\frac{\eta_{\mu\nu}}{2t}
. On these grounds,
the power series
eq.

6.16
in
tK_{i}\cdot K_{j}
obtained from integrating out the
u_{j}

order by order in
t
serves as a generating function of higher-dimensional UV divergences.
In practice, we truncate the power series and only track terms up to a maximum order
of
t
corresponding to the UV divergences up to some maximum spacetime dimension.

In the cases of a massless scalar box with
(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3},K_{4})\rightarrow(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})
and a one-mass scalar triangle with
(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3})\rightarrow(k_{12},k_{3},k_{4})
, the series expansion in the integrand of (
6.16
) yields

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{1})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{12})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{123})^{2}}\Bigg|_{\rm UV}\!=\!\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\frac{1}{6}+\frac{ts_{13}}{120}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{t^{2}}{5040}(2s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{12}s_{23}{+}2s_{23}^{2})+\frac{t^{3}s_{13}}{181440}\,(3s_{12}^{2}{-}2s_{12}s_{23}{+}3s_{23}^{2})+{\cal O}(t^{4})\bigg\}\,,

(6.18)

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{12})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{123})^{2}}\Bigg|_{\rm UV}\!=\!\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{2-D/2}\,\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{ts_{12}}{24}+\frac{t^{2}s_{12}^{2}}{360}-\frac{t^{3}s_{12}^{3}}{6720}+{\cal O}(t^{4})\bigg\}\,.

The scalar box in particular already provides an opportunity to verify
the identifications between
t
and the string moduli introduced
above. The details of the mapping will be discussed as part of the
amplitude matchings below.

An alternative method of constructing the Laurent polynomials directly
from logarithmic divergences in dimensional regularization is provided in
appendix

C
.

6.4
UV divergences from open-string effective actions

We start by matching the UV divergences of one-loop matrix elements of gauge multiplets
with the contributions to one-loop string amplitudes from the cusp
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
.
For type-I superstrings with gauge group
SO(32)
, UV divergences at one loop cancel
between cylinder and Moebius-strip worldsheets in the single-trace sector and
are regulated by closed-string exchange in the double-trace sector
Green:1987mn
. Hence, the
Laurent polynomials (
6.8
) encoding the dominant contributions at the cusp may be
viewed as the “would-be”-UV-divergences of the effective field theory that drop out from the complete one-loop
amplitude of the
SO(32)
open superstring.

6.4.1
Four points

As in section
4.1
, we omit the overall factor of

t_{8}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})
and study four-point UV divergences of the
scalar integral
\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)
in (
4.6
).
Our starting points are the Feynman integrals in
(
4.7
) and (
4.12
) as well as their higher-order counterparts
in the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion which will now be matched with Laurent polynomials in (
6.8
). It will be
natural to collect the results according to the zeta values, rather
than the
{\alpha^{\prime}}
striation of (
6.8
).

For the box integral and the scalar triangles in (
4.7
), the UV divergences
are given by (
6.3
). At the order of
\alpha^{\prime 3}\zeta_{3}
, we
additionally encounter antisymmetrized vector triangles and
bubbles on external legs
with the following UV contributions according to (
6.16
):

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\,\frac{(s_{1,\ell}{-}s_{2,\ell})}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{12})^{2}(\ell{+}k_{123})^{2}}\Bigg|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle\!=\!\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\!\mathrm{d}t\,t^{2-D/2}\,(s_{23}{-}s_{13})\,\bigg[\frac{1}{6}{-}\frac{ts_{12}}{120}{+}\frac{t^{2}s_{12}^{2}}{2520}{-}\frac{t^{3}s_{12}^{3}}{60480}{+}{\cal O}(t^{4})\bigg]

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\,\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell{+}k_{1})^{2}}\Bigg|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle\!=\!\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\!\mathrm{d}t\,t^{1-D/2}\,.

(6.19)

On these grounds, the Feynman integrals in (
4.7
) have the combined UV behaviour

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}

\displaystyle\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|_{\rm UV}=\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\bigg\{\bigg[\frac{1}{6}+\frac{ts_{13}}{120}+\frac{t^{2}(2s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{12}s_{23}{+}2s_{23}^{2})}{5040}

(6.20)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +\frac{t^{3}s_{13}(3s_{12}^{2}{-}2s_{12}s_{23}{+}3s_{23}^{2})}{181440}+\ldots\bigg]+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}\bigg[\frac{s_{13}}{t}+\frac{s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{23}^{2}}{12}-\frac{t(s_{12}^{3}{+}s_{23}^{3})}{180}+\ldots\bigg]

\displaystyle+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}\bigg[{-}\frac{6s_{13}}{t^{2}}+\frac{2(s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{12}s_{23}{+}s_{23}^{2})}{3t}+\frac{s_{13}(3s_{12}^{2}-2s_{12}s_{23}+3s_{23}^{2})}{60}+\ldots\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{4})\bigg\}\,.

Higher orders in
\alpha^{\prime}
additionally involve two-mass bubbles, tadpoles and multiple powers
of loop momenta in the numerators which can also be addressed by the techniques of
section
6.3
. At the order of
\alpha^{\prime 4}\zeta_{4}
for instance,
the numerators in (
4.15
) give rise to UV divergences

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{\rm{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4}\zeta_{4}}_{\rm UV}=\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\bigg\{\frac{12s_{13}}{t^{3}}-\frac{3s_{13}^{2}}{t^{2}}

(6.21)

\displaystyle+\frac{s_{13}(18s_{12}^{2}{-}17s_{12}s_{23}{+}18s_{23}^{2})}{12t}+\ldots\bigg\}\,.

All of these UV contributions are in perfect agreement in the Laurent polynomials in (
6.8
):
with the dictionary

\textrm{open strings:}\ \ \ t=-2\pi i\alpha^{\prime}\tau=-2i\alpha^{\prime}T\,,

(6.22)

between the purely imaginary modular parameter
\tau
of the cylinder
and the real Schwinger parameter
t
on the worldline,
25
25

25

The
dictionary (
6.22
) can be further validated by comparing
the exponent
-tk_{i}\cdot k_{j}u_{ij}^{2}
in the Schwinger
parametrization (
6.14
) of
n
-gons with the contributions

\sim e^{i\pi\alpha^{\prime}\tau s_{ij}u_{ij}^{2}}
to the open-string
Koba-Nielsen factor in the second line of (
6.8
). Moreover,
by rewriting (
6.23
) as

\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4)\,\big|_{\rm UV}=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3}(\frac{\pi}{t})^{D/2}\ldots
, the
dimension-dependence
\sim(\frac{\pi}{t})^{D/2}
on the
right-hand side is consistent with the factor of

\sqrt{-2i{\alpha^{\prime}}\tau}^{10-D}
in
(D\neq 10)
-dimensional open-string
amplitudes after loop integration.
any power of
T
at the

\alpha^{\prime\leq 3}
-orders of (
6.8
) is accurately mapped to a
UV power divergence in the effective field theory, with precise
matching of the coefficients. In other words, we have confirmed up to
and including
\alpha^{\prime 3}
that

\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|_{\rm UV}=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}\Big(1,2,3,4;\tau{=}\frac{it}{2\pi{\alpha^{\prime}}}\Big)

(6.23)

reproduces the UV regime (
6.16
) of the Schwinger parametrized one-loop matrix elements.
Note that the leading orders
\alpha^{\prime\leq 3}
in the Laurent polynomials are already
sensitive to the UV divergences due to higher
\alpha^{\prime}
-orders of the one-loop matrix
elements. For instance, the coefficients of
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{5},\,{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}
and
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}^{3}

in
a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}(1,2,3,4;\tau)
already crosscheck the numerators of

\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}
at
\alpha^{\prime 5}
(see (
4.16
), (
4.17
)) and at
\alpha^{\prime 6}

(see appendix
A.1
), respectively.
Our checks of (
6.23
) therefore include the UV divergences

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{\rm{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6}\zeta_{6}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\bigg[-\frac{20}{t^{4}}(s_{12}^{2}+4s_{12}s_{23}+s_{23}^{2})+\ldots\bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{\rm{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7}\zeta_{7}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\bigg[-\frac{48}{t^{4}}\,s_{13}(s_{12}^{2}+s_{12}s_{23}+s_{23}^{2})+\ldots\bigg]\,,

(6.24)

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{\rm{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8}\zeta_{8}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\bigg[\frac{168}{t^{5}}\,s_{13}(s_{12}^{2}+s_{12}s_{23}+s_{23}^{2})+\ldots\bigg]\,,

which occur in formally vanishing or negative numbers of spacetime dimensions
by the power of the Schwinger parameter
t
in these examples, see the discussion
around (
6.16
).

summarizes all the UV divergences that we have matched with the

.

The critical dimensions where the UV divergences of the operators in the first column
