). Dashed vertical lines separate the contributions that survive upon symmetrizing open-string amplitudes over the color orderings (left) from those that cancel upon symmetrization (right).

By imposing the well-tested correspondence (
6.23
) to hold
for higher orders of
a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}
beyond

\alpha^{\prime 3}
, one can efficiently predict the Laurent polynomials of
elliptic multiple zeta values up to the transcendentality where

\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}
is available.
We have determined the loop integrand of
\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)
up to and including the order of
\alpha^{\prime 8}
, as well as

{\alpha^{\prime}}^{9}\zeta_{9}
, giving the following new predictions for the coefficients
of the leading orders in the Laurent polynomials

\displaystyle\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8}\zeta_{3}\zeta_{5}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{-4}{t^{4}}\left(4s_{12}^{4}+11s_{12}^{3}s_{23}+6s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{2}+11s_{12}s_{23}^{3}+4s_{23}^{4}\right)+\dots

\displaystyle\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8}\zeta_{3,5}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{4}{5t^{4}}\left(10s_{12}^{4}+17s_{12}^{3}s_{23}-6s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{2}+17s_{12}s_{23}^{3}+10s_{23}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4s_{13}}{5t^{3}}(2s_{12}^{4}+5s_{12}^{3}s_{23}+10s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{2}+5s_{12}s_{23}^{3}+2s_{23}^{4})+\ldots

(6.25)

\displaystyle\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}^{2}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{2}{t^{3}}s_{12}s_{13}s_{23}\left(s_{12}^{2}+s_{23}^{2}\right)+\dots

\displaystyle\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\Big|^{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{9}\zeta_{9}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{4}{t^{5}}\left(94s_{12}^{4}+227s_{12}^{3}s_{23}+78s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{2}+227s_{12}s_{23}^{3}+94s_{23}^{4}\right)+\dots\,.

The arrows indicate that the integration measure
\pi^{D/2}\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}

has been suppressed on the right-hand sides. The methods in both
section

6.3
and

appendix

C
are straightforward to extend such that combinatorics become the limiting
factor in extracting any desired order in
t
and
s_{ij}
, which we plan to address in a future work. In this way,
integrating polynomials in proper times

u_{j}
can in principle give access to the MZVs of any weight for which
the expansion of six-point disk integrals is tractable, at arbitrary

\alpha^{\prime}
-orders of
a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}
, bypassing the degeneration limits of large numbers of
elliptic multiple zeta values.

6.4.2
Five Points

At five points, the UV expansion of the three series of Feynman integrals in (
5.38
)
to (
5.40
) determines all other cases via permutations of the external legs. The Schwinger
parametrization of these momentum-space expressions in the UV regime
perfectly lines up with the Laurent polynomials in (
6.13
),

\pi^{-D/2}\,[a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}a_{5}\,|\,E_{P}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}=-\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,[\![a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}a_{5}\,|\,E_{P}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}\,\big|_{\tau=\frac{it}{2\pi{\alpha^{\prime}}}}\,,

(6.26)

for instance

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\,[12345|E_{1|23,4,5}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{{-}\frac{1}{6s_{12}}-\frac{1}{6s_{23}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\bigg(\frac{2s_{14}}{s_{23}}+\frac{2s_{35}}{s_{12}}-3\bigg)\bigg(\frac{t}{240}+\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}}{2t}-\frac{3{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t^{2}}+\frac{6{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4}\zeta_{4}}{t^{3}}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\,[12345|E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{{-}\frac{1}{6s_{12}}

(6.27)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\bigg(1+\frac{2s_{35}}{s_{12}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{t}{240}+\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}}{2t}-\frac{3{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t^{2}}+\frac{6{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4}\zeta_{4}}{t^{3}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(s_{ij})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\,[12345|E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\frac{k_{5}^{\mu}}{6s_{15}}{-}\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}}{6s_{12}}{+}\bigg(k_{35}^{\mu}-k_{24}^{\mu}+\frac{2s_{35}}{s_{12}}k_{2}^{\mu}-\frac{2s_{24}}{s_{15}}k_{5}^{\mu}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\bigg(\frac{t}{240}+\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}}{2t}-\frac{3{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t^{2}}+\frac{6{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4}\zeta_{4}}{t^{3}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(s_{ij})\bigg\}\,.

The full one-loop matrix element can be assembled from the loop
integrals in
[12345|E_{P}\rangle
together with the

t_{8}
-tensors as prescribed in (
5.33
). For the scalars

t_{8}(12,3,4,5)
and vectors
t_{+}^{\mu}(1,2,3,4,5)
defined in
(
4.29
), the linearized gauge transformations are detailed in
(
4.35
). At each order in
t
and
\alpha^{\prime}
, the Schwinger
integrand resulting from (
6.27
) is a gauge invariant
combination of
t_{8}
-tensors which can be expressed in terms of

A_{\rm SYM}^{\rm tree}
by repeated use of (
4.38
) and

\displaystyle\frac{t_{8}(12,3,4,5)}{s_{12}}+\text{cyc}(1,2,3)=s_{45}\Big[s_{34}A_{\rm SYM}^{\rm tree}(1,2,3,4,5)-s_{24}A_{\rm SYM}^{\rm tree}(1,3,2,4,5)\Big]\,.

(6.28)

By furthermore reducing the SYM trees to
the BCJ basis
\{A_{\rm SYM}^{\rm tree}(1{,}2{,}3{,}4{,}5),A_{\rm SYM}^{\rm tree}(1{,}3{,}2{,}4{,}5)\}
,
each order in the Schwinger integrands of
A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4,5)

can be characterized by a
2\times 2
matrix with
s_{ij}
-dependent entries acting
on the two-component vector of
A_{\rm SYM}^{\rm tree}
,

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4,5)\\
A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,3,2,4,5)\end{array}\right)\Bigg|_{\rm UV}=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\frac{1}{6}P_{2}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\bigg(\frac{t}{120}+\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}\zeta_{2}}{t}-\frac{6{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t^{2}}+\frac{12{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4}\zeta_{4}}{t^{3}}\bigg)M_{3}+{\cal O}(s_{ij}^{4})\bigg\}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1,2,3,4,5)\\
A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1,3,2,4,5)\end{array}\right)\,.

The entries of the
2\times 2
matrices

and
M_{w}
are degree-
w
polynomials in

with
rational coefficients
Schlotterer:2012ny
, for instance

P_{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}s_{12}s_{34}{-}s_{34}s_{45}{-}s_{15}s_{12}&s_{13}s_{24}\\
s_{12}s_{34}&s_{13}s_{24}{-}s_{24}s_{45}{-}s_{15}s_{13}\end{array}\right)\,.

(6.33)

The cases in (
6.4.2
) are known from the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion of five-point disk integrals,
and their explicit form at
w\leq 22
can be downloaded from
MZVWebsite
. The leading term
in (
6.4.2
) arises from

\frac{t_{8}(12,3,4,5)}{s_{12}}+{\rm cyc}(1,2,3,4,5)=-\Big[(P_{2})_{11}A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1,2,3,4,5)+(P_{2})_{12}A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1,3,2,4,5)\Big]\,,

(6.34)

which is the representation of
Mafra:2011nv
for the color-ordered tree-level matrix element with
a single-insertion of the effective operator
\alpha^{\prime 2}\zeta_{2}{\rm Tr}(F^{4})
. Higher orders
of (

) will involve additional
2\times 2
matrices beyond the
P_{w}
and
M_{w}

from genus-zero integrals, starting with
L_{4}
in section 5.1 of
Broedel:2014vla
.

6.5
UV divergences from closed-string effective actions

Closed strings feature a similar correspondence between UV divergences in the effective field
theory and Laurent polynomials in the expansion of torus integrals around the cusp. The closed-string
amplitude is free of UV divergences as can be understood from the bound
\Im(\tau)\geq\sqrt{3}/2
for
the modular parameters
\tau\in\mathfrak{F}
in (
5.1
): by the shape of the fundamental
domain of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, modular invariance
imposes an UV cutoff on the Schwinger parameter
t
which is absent for the Feynman integrals in
the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion of the matrix elements
M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm eff}}
.

6.5.1
Four points

We again peel off a global factor of
|t_{8}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})|^{2}
and discuss four-point gravitational one-loop matrix
elements at the level of the scalar integrals
\widehat{M}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}
in (
4.18
).
Based on standard Schwinger parametrizations of the Feynman integrals in (
4.20
), we find

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}

\displaystyle\widehat{M}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}\,\Big|_{\rm UV}=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\bigg[1+\frac{t^{2}}{360}(s_{12}^{2}{-}s_{13}s_{23})-\frac{t^{3}s_{12}s_{13}s_{23}}{6048}+\ldots\bigg]

(6.35)

\displaystyle\ \ +{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}\bigg[\frac{8}{t}(s_{12}^{2}{-}s_{13}s_{23})-s_{12}s_{13}s_{23}+\ldots\bigg]+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5}\zeta_{5}\bigg[{-}\frac{60s_{12}s_{13}s_{23}}{t^{2}}+\ldots\bigg]+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{6})\bigg\}\,.

At higher order
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6}\zeta_{3}^{2}
and
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7}\zeta_{7}
, the numerators in (

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{M}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}\,\Big|^{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6}\zeta_{3}^{2}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\frac{32\sigma_{4}}{t^{2}}-\frac{8\sigma_{5}}{t}+\frac{12\sigma_{6}+\sigma_{3}^{2}}{15}-\frac{43\sigma_{7}}{630}\,t+\ldots\bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{M}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}\,\Big|^{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7}\zeta_{7}}_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\frac{128\sigma_{4}}{t^{3}}-\frac{98\sigma_{5}}{t^{2}}-\frac{36\sigma_{6}-46\sigma_{3}^{2}}{5t}

(6.36)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\frac{89\sigma_{7}}{210}+\frac{1959\sigma_{8}+121\sigma_{4}^{2}}{18900}\,t+\ldots\bigg\}\,,

where the Mandelstam variables are organized in terms of the symmetric polynomials

\sigma_{k}=\frac{1}{k}\,(s_{12}^{k}+s_{23}^{k}+s_{13}^{k})\,.

Similar to the open-string discussion in the previous section, we compare the

\textrm{closed strings:}\ \ \ t=4\pi{\alpha^{\prime}}\Im(\tau)=4{\alpha^{\prime}}y\,.

Finally, the comparison of UV divergences of gauge and gravitational one-loop matrix elements offers
a new perspective on the single-valued map (
6.10
) between Laurent polynomials of elliptic
multiple zeta values and modular graph forms. Based on tree-level results,
\widehat{M}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}

and the symmetrized version of
\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}(1,2,3,4)

\pi\tau\rightarrow 2\pi i\Im\tau

) between
\widehat{M}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}

and
\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\textrm{eff}}
implies the connection
(
6.10
) between Laurent polynomials of elliptic
multiple zeta values and modular graph forms.

6.5.2
Five Points

We continue the matching at five points, analyzing the pairings

\langle E_{Q}|E_{P}\rangle
in (
5.32
). We have verified that the
Schwinger parametrizations of the Feynman integrals match the Laurent
polynomials of the torus integrals

\langle\!\langle E_{Q}|E_{P}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}
in
(

). For instance, the leading orders in the UV expansions
of the results in section

such as

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\,\langle E_{1|23,4,5}|E_{1|24,3,5}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{{-}\frac{1}{s_{12}}

\displaystyle+\bigg(\frac{t^{2}}{720}+\frac{4{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{2(s_{35}s_{45}-s_{34}^{2})}{s_{12}}-s_{12}+5s_{34}\bigg)+\ldots\bigg\}

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\,\langle E_{1|23,4,5}|E_{1|45,2,3}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{t^{2}}{720}{+}\frac{4{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t}\bigg)(s_{24}{+}s_{35}{-}s_{25}{-}s_{34})+\ldots\bigg\}

\displaystyle\pi^{-D/2}\,\langle E_{1|23,4,5}|E^{\mu}_{1|2,3,4,5}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t_{\rm max}}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\bigg\{\frac{k_{3}^{\mu}}{s_{13}}-\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}}{s_{12}}+\bigg(\frac{t^{2}}{720}+\frac{4{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t}\bigg)

(6.42)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \times\bigg(k_{3}^{\mu}\frac{(s_{24}^{2}{+}s_{25}^{2}{+}s_{45}^{2})}{s_{13}}-k_{2}^{\mu}\frac{(s_{34}^{2}{+}s_{35}^{2}{+}s_{45}^{2})}{s_{12}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +(k_{3}^{\mu}{-}k_{2}^{\mu})s_{45}+k_{4}^{\mu}(s_{24}{-}s_{34})+k_{5}^{\mu}(s_{25}{-}s_{35})\bigg)+\ldots\bigg\}

are related to the degenerate torus integrals in (

)
according to the general dictionary

\pi^{-D/2}\,\langle E_{Q}|E_{P}\rangle\,\Big|_{\rm UV}=-\int^{t_{\rm max}}_{0}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{3-D/2}\,\langle\!\langle E_{Q}|E_{P}\rangle\!\rangle_{\tau}\,\big|_{\Im\tau=\frac{t}{4\pi{\alpha^{\prime}}}}\,,

see (

The one-loop matrix element

is given
in terms of

The coefficients of

;

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{t_{8}(12,3,4,5)\bar{t}_{8}(12,3,4,5)}{s_{12}}+(1{,}2|1{,}2{,}3{,}4{,}5)\bigg]+\frac{1}{2}t^{\mu}_{+}(1,2,3,4,5)\eta_{\mu\nu}\bar{t}^{\nu}_{+}(1,2,3,4,5)\,\big|_{\rm IIB}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1{,}2{,}3{,}5{,}4)\\

\frac{D{-}8}{6}&:\ \phi_{D}h^{4}\end{array}\right.\,,

where the

\displaystyle M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{5,{\rm eff}}\,\Big|_{\rm UV}^{{\rm IIB},h^{5}}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1{,}2{,}3{,}5{,}4)\\

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ -\bigg(\frac{t^{3}}{6048}+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}+\frac{60{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5}\zeta_{5}}{t^{2}}\bigg)M_{3}^{2}+{\cal O}(s_{ij}^{7})\bigg]\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1{,}2{,}3{,}4{,}5)\\

. For R-symmetry violating type-IIB states such as

\displaystyle M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{5,{\rm eff}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\frac{D{-}8}{6}M_{3}+\frac{D{-}12}{5}\bigg(\frac{t^{2}}{720}+\frac{4{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}}{t}\bigg)M_{5}

(6.55)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{D{-}14}{12}\bigg(\frac{t^{3}}{6048}+{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{3}+\frac{60{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5}\zeta_{5}}{t^{2}}\bigg)M_{3}^{2}+{\cal O}(s_{ij}^{7})\bigg]\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1{,}2{,}3{,}4{,}5)\\
A^{\textrm{tree}}_{\rm SYM}(1{,}3{,}2{,}4{,}5)\end{array}\right)\,.

The
D
-dimensional relative factors in comparison to (
6.5.2
)
play an important role for the S-duality properties of type-IIB string
amplitudes
Green:2013bza
;
Mafra:2015mja
;
Green:2019rhz
;
DHoker:2020tcq
. From a supergravity point of view, the factors of

D{-}8,\ D{-}12
and
D{-}14
in (
6.55
) prevent
R-symmetry violation through the respective UV divergence in
8,12
and
14

spacetime dimensions
Mafra:2015mja
;
Pioline:2018pso
;
DHoker:2020tcq
.

7
Comparison with non-analytic terms in string amplitudes

On top of the analysis of UV divergences in the previous section, we can also
relate the discontinuities of one-loop matrix elements to string-theory computations.
Unitarity at the level of the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion implies that the discontinuities of
one-loop string amplitudes in
s_{ij}
agree with those of
A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm eff}}(1,2,\ldots,n)

and
M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm eff}}
. We shall first confirm this for the four-point closed-string
results in the literature and then spell out predictions for the non-analytic sector of one-loop
four-point open-string amplitudes.

7.1
Reproducing non-analytic terms of closed superstrings

The non-analytic part of the four-point one-loop amplitude of type-II
superstrings is known to all orders in
\alpha^{\prime}

DHoker:2019blr
, also see
Green:1999pv
;
Green:2008uj
;
DHoker:2015gmr
for earlier results at the leading

\alpha^{\prime}
-orders. Based on the Feynman integrals in section

4.2
, we find the following logarithmic behaviour in the
low-energy expansion

\displaystyle M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}\,\big|_{\rm disc}

\displaystyle=M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm SUGRA}}\,\big|_{\rm disc}+|t_{8}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})|^{2}\bigg\{{-}\frac{\alpha^{\prime 4}\zeta_{3}}{45}s_{12}^{4}+\frac{\alpha^{\prime 6}\zeta_{5}}{1260}s_{12}^{4}(s_{13}s_{23}{-}22s_{12}^{2})

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+\frac{\alpha^{\prime 7}\zeta_{3}^{2}}{1260}s_{12}^{5}(12s_{12}^{2}+s_{13}s_{23})-\frac{\alpha^{\prime 8}\zeta_{7}}{18900}s_{12}^{4}(260s_{12}^{4}-25s_{12}^{2}s_{13}s_{23}+2s_{13}^{2}s_{23}^{2})

(7.1)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+\frac{\alpha^{\prime 9}\zeta_{3}\zeta_{5}}{4725}s_{12}^{5}(70s_{12}^{4}+5s_{12}^{2}s_{13}s_{23}-s_{13}^{2}s_{23}^{2})+{\cal O}(\alpha^{\prime 10})\bigg\}\log\left(\frac{s_{12}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+\text{cyc}(2,3,4)\,,

where
\big|_{\rm disc}
instructs to disregard any analytic dependence on
\alpha^{\prime}
. In this way,
the polynomials in
s_{ij}
introduced by a change of the scale
\mu
do not affect the terms under
consideration, see
section

C.4
for the origin of the terms
\log(\frac{s_{ij}}{\mu^{2}})
. To the
orders shown, (
7.1
) exactly matches the non-analytic terms of
DHoker:2019blr

obtained from the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansions in Theorem 4.1 in version 2 of the reference.
27
27

27

We are
grateful to Eric D’Hoker and Michael Green for correspondence on a factor of 4 that was
changed since the first version of
DHoker:2019blr
.

A naive power-series expansion of the integrals (
6.4
) over closed-string punctures
cannot detect the logarithmic dependence on
s_{ij}
. Still, the divergent
\tau
-integrals over
certain modular graph forms in the
\alpha^{\prime}
-expansion of (
6.4
) have crucial information
on the non-analytic sector of one-loop closed-string amplitudes, see

Green:1999pv
;
Green:2008uj
;
DHoker:2015gmr
;
DHoker:2019blr
for further details.

7.2
Predicting non-analytic terms of open superstrings

As an open-string counterpart of (
7.1
), we can
assemble the discontinuities of the Feynman integrals in the
four-point one-loop matrix element of section
4.1
,

\displaystyle A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm eff}}(1,2,3,4)\,\big|_{\rm disc}=A^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm SYM}}(1,2,3,4)\,\big|_{\rm disc}

(7.2)

\displaystyle\ \ \ +t_{8}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})\bigg\{f(s_{12},s_{23})\log\left(\frac{s_{12}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+f(s_{23},s_{12})\log\left(\frac{s_{23}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\bigg\}\,,

where
28
28

28

We are grateful to Lorenz Eberhardt and Sebastian Mizera for
pointing out typos in the exponents of
s_{12}
and
s_{23}
in the coefficient
of
\zeta_{5}
which we corrected in the present version.

\displaystyle f(s_{12},

\displaystyle s_{23})=\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{3}\zeta_{2}}{180}s_{12}^{3}-\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{4}\zeta_{3}}{1260}s_{12}^{3}(4s_{12}{+}s_{23})+\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{5}\zeta_{2}^{2}}{12600}s_{12}^{3}(46s_{12}^{2}{-}2s_{12}s_{23}{+}s_{23}^{2})

\displaystyle-\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}}{5040}s_{12}^{4}(12s_{12}^{2}{+}s_{13}s_{23})-\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{6}\zeta_{5}}{15120}s_{12}^{3}(38s_{12}^{3}{+}12s_{12}^{2}s_{23}{+}3s_{12}s_{23}^{2}{+}s_{23}^{3})

\displaystyle+\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7}\zeta_{3}^{2}}{30240}s_{12}^{4}(20s_{12}^{3}{+}2s_{12}^{2}s_{23}{-}3s_{12}s_{23}^{2}{-}s_{23}^{3})

\displaystyle+\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{7}\zeta_{2}^{3}}{5292000}s_{12}^{3}(12320s_{12}^{4}{-}275s_{12}^{3}s_{23}{+}357s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{2}{-}36s_{12}s_{23}^{3}{+}32s_{23}^{4})

(7.3)

\displaystyle-\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8}\zeta_{7}}{831600}s_{12}^{3}(1660s_{12}^{5}{+}645s_{12}^{4}s_{23}{+}296s_{12}^{3}s_{23}^{2}{+}137s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{3}{+}36s_{12}s_{23}^{4}{+}10s_{23}^{5})

\displaystyle-\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8}\zeta_{2}^{2}\zeta_{3}}{756000}s_{12}^{4}(1200s_{12}^{4}{-}115s_{12}^{3}s_{23}{-}48s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{2}{-}s_{12}s_{23}^{3}-8s_{23}^{4})

\displaystyle-\frac{{\alpha^{\prime}}^{8}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{5}}{37800}s_{12}^{4}(70s_{12}^{4}{-}5s_{12}^{3}s_{23}{-}6s_{12}^{2}s_{23}^{2}{-}2s_{12}s_{23}^{3}{-}s_{23}^{4})+{\cal O}({\alpha^{\prime}}^{9})\,.

This sets our prediction for the non-analytic terms in the planar-cylinder contribution to the four-point
one-loop open-string amplitude.
In addition to the above terms, we note that the
\zeta_{3,5}

integrand only contains external bubbles (i.e. Figure
3b
) and
tadpoles, which integrate to zero in dimensional reduction. As such,
there are no terms of the form
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{9}\zeta_{3,5}\log(\frac{s_{ij}}{\mu^{2}})
as required
by unitarity (there is no
\zeta_{3,5}
in the four-point tree-level amplitude).
Again, the logarithms in
s_{ij}
can be anticipated from the divergent

\tau
integrals in a naive power-series expansion of the integrals
over the open-string punctures (with elliptic multiple zeta values in the
\tau
-integrand).
With the notable exception at the subleading
\alpha^{\prime}
-order discussed
in section 4.2 of
Hohenegger:2017kqy
, the interplay between the analytic and non-analytic sector
of one-loop open-string amplitudes has not yet been studied at the level of explicit higher-order results.

8
Conclusion and outlook

In this work, we have used a confluence of ideas from ambitwistor, conventional string and (effective) field
theories to obtain one-loop matrix elements of higher-mass-dimension operators

D^{2k}F^{n}
and
D^{2k}R^{n}
. Our method applies to the supersymmetric
operators in the tree-level low-energy effective actions of type-I and type-II superstrings
and is driven by the forward limits of the disk and sphere integrals in massless tree
amplitudes. Both the one-loop matrix elements and their tree-level building blocks are
organized in a series expansion in the inverse string tension
\alpha^{\prime}
. Moreover, one
can further distinguish different operators at the same mass dimension from the
conjecturally
\mathbb{Q}
-linearly independent multiple zeta values in their coefficients.

Our construction passes a variety of consistency checks including a matching of the matrix elements’ UV
divergences and discontinuities with expectations based on one-loop superstring amplitudes.

•

The UV divergences of the loop integrals associated with various
\alpha^{\prime}
-orders and
spacetime dimensions line up with the degeneration limits of genus-one integrals over torus or cylinder
punctures. This was confirmed in all cases we considered, and by imposing consistency at higher orders,
our proposal offers a new window into the expansion of modular graph forms
and elliptic multiple zeta values around the cusp.

•

The discontinuities of the one-loop matrix elements in this work via logarithmic dependence on Mandelstam invariants reproduce those of the one-loop four-graviton amplitude of type II superstrings (which is verified up to and including the order of
\alpha^{\prime 9}

DHoker:2019blr
). For open strings at
\geq 4
points and closed strings at
\geq 5
points, our one-loop matrix elements encode new results on the non-analytic sectors of one-loop string amplitudes.

By their construction from genus-zero integrals, the
one-loop matrix elements evidently enjoy an echo of the monodromy relations

Plahte:1970wy
;
BjerrumBohr:2009rd
;
Stieberger:2009hq

and KLT relations
Kawai:1985xq
of open- and closed-string tree amplitudes, respectively.
These properties are spelt out for the loop integrand of
n
-point matrix elements
and furnish an
\alpha^{\prime}
-uplift of one-loop BCJ and KLT relations
among field-theory amplitudes in the formulation of
He:2016mzd
;
He:2017spx
. The
monodromy and KLT relations of one-loop matrix elements apply to the representation of their
loop integrand where the inverse Feynman propagators are linearized
in loop momenta as familiar from ambitwistor strings
Geyer:2015bja
;
Geyer:2015jch
.
It would be interesting to further explore the practical implications of the one-loop monodromy relations of
open-string amplitudes as given in
Tourkine:2016bak
;
Hohenegger:2017kqy
;
Ochirov:2017jby
;
Tourkine:2019ukp
;
Casali:2019ihm
;
Casali:2020knc
;
Stieberger:2021daa
, as well as tentative KLT relations between one-loop open- and closed-string amplitudes that do not rely on linearized propagators, or that hold after integration.

Monodromy and KLT relations of one-loop matrix elements reflect the underlying
color-kinematics duality and double-copy properties of effective-field-theory operators,
order by order in
\alpha^{\prime}
. This suggests an important line of follow-up work to manifest a one-loop cubic-diagram formulation of these properties. In particular, it would be very interesting to construct kinematic numerators at higher orders in
\alpha^{\prime}
that obey Jacobi identities on quadratic Feynman propagators. The numerators due to higher-derivative operators will involve
large numbers of loop momenta and thereby serve as a useful laboratory for the rich
loop-momentum dependence of field-theory numerators at higher loop orders. It is plausible that
one-loop matrix elements may even provide new input on the difficulties in finding local numerators
with kinematic Jacobi relations for the five-loop integrand of maximal supergravity

Bern:2017yxu
;
Bern:2017ucb
.

One-loop matrix elements at four and five points are spelt out to various orders in
\alpha^{\prime}
,
with all-multiplicity conjectures for the
\alpha^{\prime\leq 3}
orders of certain scalar toy integrals.
An obvious open problem for the future concerns explicit representations of higher-point matrix
elements. Similarly, our proposal for non-planar or double-trace matrix elements of gauge multiplets
calls for explicit examples and consistency checks.
Particularly interesting features can be expected at six points: first, our method qualifies for
explicit investigations of
\alpha^{\prime}
-corrections to the one-loop hexagon anomaly of ten-dimensional
super-Yang-Mills
Frampton:1983ah
;
Frampton:1983nr
and its cancellation for type-I
superstrings
Green:1984sg
;
Green:1984qs
. Second,
one-loop six-point amplitudes in field theory pose additional challenges
Mafra:2014gja
;
Bridges:2021ebs
in manifesting the
color-kinematics duality and double copy as compared
to
\leq 5
points, at least in
D=10
dimensions and through
quadratic Feynman propagators
He:2017spx
.
More specifically, while a four-dimensional construction have been understood for some time
Bjerrum-Bohr:2013iza
, it remains to iron out details of the double copy for the SYM loop integrand recently obtained in
Bridges:2021ebs
.

The one-loop matrix elements in this work incorporate diagrams with single and multiple insertions
of higher-mass-dimension operators, see e.g. Figure
1
. In the open-string case, diagrams with single insertions
encode the zero-momentum limit of maximally supersymmetric one-loop form factors involving the respective
{\rm Tr}\{D^{2k}F^{n}\}

operator. Supersymmetric form factors have prominently featured in the recent amplitudes literature, for instance
through their construction via unitarity-based methods
Brandhuber:2010ad
;
Brandhuber:2011tv
;
Bork:2011cj
;
Bork:2012tt
;
Engelund:2012re
;
Nandan:2014oga
;
Bianchi:2018peu
or their color-kinematics duality
Boels:2012ew
;
Yang:2016ear
;
Lin:2021kht
.

We expect fruitful connections between the one-loop matrix elements in this work
and stringy canonical forms
Arkani-Hamed:2019mrd
;
Arkani-Hamed:2019plo
;
Arkani-Hamed:2020tuz
.
For instance, the
\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
limits of stringy canonical forms for finite-type cluster
algebras produce the integrands for one-loop tadpole diagrams in case of type B, C and the
one-loop planar
\phi^{3}
diagrams in case of type D. It would be interesting to compare the

\alpha^{\prime}
-expansions of such stringy canonical forms with the forward limits of disk and sphere
integrals that carry the
\alpha^{\prime}
-dependence in our results. Moreover, our results together with
follow-up studies of analytic contributions to one-loop string amplitudes may lead to interesting
connections with the EFT-hedron
Arkani-Hamed:2020blm
.
Relatedly, it will be rewarding to study a loop-level formulation of the recent advances
in finding new double-copy constructions at higher mass dimension
Carrasco:2019yyn
;
Carrasco:2021ptp
;
Chi:2021mio
and to compare with the one-loop matrix elements in this work.

Finally, it would also be rewarding to investigate similar proposals for
matrix elements at two loops and beyond. A promising starting point is
offered by the ambitwistor-string construction of two-loop
field-theory amplitudes via bi-nodal Riemann spheres

Geyer:2016wjx
;
Gomez:2016cqb
;
Geyer:2018xwu
;
Geyer:2019hnn
,
also see
Geyer:2021oox
for an impressive three-loop result in
this framework. It is conceivable that suitable

\alpha^{\prime}
-uplifts via double-forward limits of disk and sphere
integrals yield two-loop matrix elements of
D^{2k}F^{n}
and

D^{2k}R^{n}
that resonate with string-theory expectations. The main
crosschecks would concern the discontinuity structure of two-loop
string amplitudes
Green:2010sp
;
Pioline:2018pso
and the
tropical degeneration of genus-two modular graph forms

Tourkine:2013rda
;
Pioline:2015qha
;
DHoker:2017pvk
;
DHoker:2018mys
;
Pioline:2018pso
;
DHoker:2020tcq
. Conversely, matrix
elements of effective operators beyond one loop have enormous
potential to infer physical and mathematical information on multiloop
string amplitudes.

(A.8)

\displaystyle N^{(\zeta_{3,5})}_{\text{bubble}}(1,234)=\frac{s_{13}}{2560}\Bigg(s_{\ell,1}^{3}\left[16s_{\ell,2}(s_{12}+s_{23})+2s_{\ell,3}(5s_{12}+3s_{23})+3\left(s_{12}^{2}-s_{23}^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+3s_{\ell,1}^{2}\bigg[s_{\ell,2}\left(2s_{\ell,3}[5s_{12}+3s_{23}]+3\left[s_{12}^{2}-s_{23}^{2}\right]\right)+8s_{\ell,2}^{2}(s_{12}+s_{23})

\displaystyle\quad+s_{\ell,3}\left(5s_{12}s_{23}+5s_{12}^{2}+2s_{23}^{2}\right)+5s_{12}^{2}s_{23}+5s_{12}s_{23}^{2}+s_{12}^{3}-2s_{23}s_{\ell,3}^{2}+s_{23}^{3}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+5s_{12}^{2}s_{23}+5s_{12}s_{23}^{2}+s_{12}^{3}-2s_{23}s_{\ell,3}^{2}+s_{23}^{3}\Big)+16s_{\ell,2}^{3}(s_{12}+s_{23})-2s_{\ell,3}^{3}(5s_{12}+2s_{23})

Appendix B
Gravitational one-loop matrix element from closed strings

In this appendix, we sketch the closed-string analogue of the discussion in
section
5.1
, where one-loop matrix elements of gauge multiplets were recovered from the

\tau\rightarrow i\infty
contributions to open-string amplitudes. The key idea for the gravitational
counterparts is to decompose the expression (
5.1
) for closed-string one-loop amplitudes
in direct analogy with (
5.12
),

M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm closed}}=\int\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell\int_{{\mathfrak{F}}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\tau\,e^{-4\pi\alpha^{\prime}\Im\tau\,\ell^{2}}\,\Big\{\underbrace{{\cal F}(\ell,\tau)-{\cal F}(\ell,i\infty)}_{(i)}+\underbrace{{\cal F}(\ell,i\infty)}_{(ii)}\Big\}\,,

(B.1)

where the following quantity no longer depends on
\ell^{2}

{\cal F}(\ell,\tau)=\int_{{\mathfrak{T}}^{n-1}_{\tau}}\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{n}\,e^{4\pi\alpha^{\prime}\Im\tau\,\ell^{2}}\,\big|{\cal K}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\,{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\big|^{2}\,.

(B.2)

We focus on the dominant contribution
(ii)
, where the worldsheet correlator and the integrals
over the punctures are evaluated on a degenerate torus. From the leftover integral over

\tau
in the expression (
B.1
) for
M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm closed}}\,|_{(ii)}
,
one can extract

\int_{{\mathfrak{F}}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\tau\,e^{-4\pi\alpha^{\prime}\Im\tau\ell^{2}}=\frac{1}{4\pi{\alpha^{\prime}}\ell^{2}}+{\cal O}((\ell^{2})^{0})

(B.3)

as the dominant contribution from the cusp, see (
5.13
) for the open-string counterpart.
By largely following the steps in (
5.14
),
the quantity
{\cal F}(\ell,i\infty)
will now be related to the loop integrand in the proposal
(
3.2
) for gravitational one-loop matrix elements.

Based on the change of variables (
5.6
) and the relation (
5.9
) between chiral integrands
and half integrands of the ambitwistor string, we can rewrite

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\rightarrow i\infty}{\cal F}(\ell,\tau)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{1}{(4\pi^{2})^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{n-1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{n}}{|\sigma_{2}\ldots\sigma_{n}|^{2}}\,\lim_{\tau\rightarrow i\infty}e^{4\pi\alpha^{\prime}\Im\tau\ell^{2}}\,\big|{\cal K}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\,{\cal J}_{n}(\ell,\tau)\big|^{2}

(B.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi^{2})^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{n-1}}\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{n}\,|{\cal I}_{n}(\ell)|^{2}\prod_{j=1}^{n}|\sigma_{j+}|^{4{\alpha^{\prime}}\ell\cdot k_{j}}\prod_{1\leq i<j}^{n}|\sigma_{ij}|^{4{\alpha^{\prime}}k_{i}\cdot k_{j}}\,.

In the parametrization of the torus worldsheet through the parallelogram with corners
-\frac{\tau}{2}
,

-\frac{\tau}{2}{+}1,\,\frac{\tau}{2}{+}1
and
\frac{\tau}{2}
, the limit
\tau\rightarrow i\infty

recovers
\mathbb{C}^{n-1}
from the integration domain in (
B.2
).

As a next step, we insert the Parke-Taylor decomposition (
2.8
) of the ambitwistor-string correlators
into (
B.4
) and identify the sphere integrals defined in (
2.23
),

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\rightarrow i\infty}{\cal F}(\ell,\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi^{2})^{3}}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{{\alpha^{\prime}}}\bigg)^{n-1}\sum_{\rho,\gamma\in S_{n-1}}N_{+|\rho|-}\bar{N}_{+|\gamma|-}\lim_{k_{\pm}\rightarrow\pm\ell}J(+,\gamma,-|+,\rho,-)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi^{2})^{3}}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{{\alpha^{\prime}}}\bigg)^{n-1}M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm eff}}\,.

(B.5)

In summary, the above assumptions and the restriction to the singular term
\ell^{-2}

in (
B.3
) lead to a tentative string origin

M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm closed}}\,\big|_{(ii)}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{8}}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{{\alpha^{\prime}}}\bigg)^{n}M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm eff}}+{\rm analytic}

(B.6)

of gravitational one-loop matrix elements (
3.2
). Regular terms
(\ell^{2})^{\geq 0}

in (
B.3
) may additionally introduce analytic contributions to (
B.6
), i.e. without any branch cuts corresponding to massless particles similar to the ones in part
(i)
of (
B.1
).

Similar to the partitioning of open-string amplitudes in (
5.12
), gravitational one-loop matrix elements
have been formally recovered from the terms
(ii)
in the closed-string amplitude (
B.1
)
which gather the dominant contributions from
\tau\rightarrow i\infty
. This is the boundary
of moduli space where the torus degenerates to a nodal sphere and the origin of all the poles in

\ell^{2}
. Conversely, the one-loop matrix elements do
not account for the terms
(i)
of closed-string amplitudes and the regular terms in (
B.3
) which are free
of massless quadratic propagators
(\ell+K)^{-2}
(with external momenta
K
) after
integration over the punctures.
Hence,
M^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{n,{\rm eff}}
is insensitive to the analytic
dependence of closed-string one-loop amplitudes that arises from

(i)
and the regular terms in (
B.3
) and plays a crucial role for the one-loop effective action of
type-II theories.

Appendix C
Laurent series from logarithmic divergences

In this appendix, we discuss an alternative approach to constructing the
Laurent polynomials in the Schwinger parameter
t
from
section

6.3
via direct
momentum-space calculations of logarithmic UV divergences in dimensional reduction. The following integrals have been used for the one-loop four and five-point matrix elements,

bubble:

\displaystyle I_{2}(k)=\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{\epsilon\gamma_{\rm E}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell+k)^{2}}\,,

(C.1a)

one-mass triangle:

\displaystyle I_{3}(k_{1},k_{2})=\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{\epsilon\gamma_{\rm E}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell+k_{1})^{2}(\ell+k_{12})^{2}}\,,

(C.1b)

two-mass triangle:

\displaystyle I_{3}(k_{1},k_{2}{+}k_{3})=\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{\epsilon\gamma_{\rm E}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell+k_{1})^{2}(\ell+k_{123})^{2}}\,,

(C.1c)

one-mass box:

\displaystyle I_{4}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{\epsilon\gamma_{\rm E}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{\ell^{2}(\ell+k_{1})^{2}(\ell+k_{12})^{2}(\ell+k_{123})^{2}}\,,

(C.1d)

where
\mu^{2}
is an arbitrary mass scale that compensates the dimension shift
D=\mathcal{D}-2\epsilon
such that the mass dimension of the integral is not changed, and
e^{\epsilon\gamma_{\rm E}}
factor absorbs all the Euler constant
\gamma_{\rm E}
in the
\epsilon
-expansion. All the
k_{i}
in (
C.1b
) to (
C.1d
)
are lightlike, while the momentum in
I_{2}
is in general off-shell, i.e.
k^{2}\neq 0
. We recall that the
signature of the spacetime metric is
(-,+,+,\ldots,+)
and the integration measure

\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell=-i\mathrm{d}\ell^{0}\,\mathrm{d}\ell^{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\ell^{D-1}

is taken to absorb the factor of
i
which appears in the more common conventions for Feynman integrals.

C.1
Logarithmic in one dimension is power in another

The first point to make is what the section title says: UV power
divergences in a specific dimension,
\mathcal{D}
, can be probed
using logarithmic UV divergences in other dimensions. This idea is
well known, if not often discussed, in the amplitudes and QFT
communities. However, since the reader may not be familiar with this
idea, we present a brief argument for it here.

The idea is most obvious when considering the Schwinger representation
of
eq.

6.14
and
eq.

6.16
where, in
D_{\text{crit}}=\mathcal{D}
, an integrand that
carries
t^{-1}
will produce the logarithm divergence in

\mathcal{D}
while all other powers of
t
will contribute to power
divergences. However, from the point of view of the
t
integration,
there is nothing special about the choice of
D
in integrand
prefactor of
t^{n-D/2}
. If we look at the coefficient of
t^{-1+m}

within the
t
integration, in addition to interpreting the term as
sourcing a power divergence we could instead think of
m
as shifting
the dimension away from
\mathcal{D}
. Running this logic in reverse,
we can find the coefficient of
t^{-1+m}
in
\mathcal{D}
dimensions
by evaluating the logarithmic divergence of the same
momentum-space integrand in
D_{\text{crit}}=\mathcal{D}+2m

dimensions instead. Importantly, this should be thought of as an
integration trick and not an actual change to the underlying theory:
we are not manipulating the dimension the theory and its states live
in, merely exploring the analytic structure of the loop integral.

C.2
Momentum-space perspective on UV divergences

We now present an alternate method for directly extracting the
logarithmic UV divergence in a given dimension directly from the
momentum-space integral, which is the one-loop analogue of the methods
employed in many studies of multi-loop super-Yang-Mills and
supergravity (see

Bern:2013qca
;
Bern:2012uf
;
Johansson:2017bfl
;
Bern:2018jmv
for a
small sample of these studies). We begin by scaling the loop momenta
via
\ell\to\beta\ell
and series expanding the integrand near

\beta\to\infty
. The resulting expressions will contain only powers
of
\ell^{2}
in the denominator, with all of the
\ell\cdot k_{i}

relegated to the numerator. Such terms require only no-scale tensor
reduction, e.g.
\ell^{\mu}\ell^{\nu}\to\frac{\ell^{2}}{D}\eta^{\mu\nu}
. After the
reduction, the numerators
n_{\lambda}
will be free of
\ell
and the denominators
will be powers
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}
of
\ell^{2}
,

\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}I(\ell)\Bigg|_{\text{UV}}\to\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\sum_{\lambda}\frac{\bar{n}_{\lambda}}{\beta^{2\lambda}(\ell^{2})^{\lambda}}\Bigg|_{\text{UV}}\,,

(C.2)

where
I(\ell)
refers to a generic loop integrand,
and the
\bar{n}_{\lambda}
are polynomials in the external momenta. In dimensional
regularization, integrals of this form are set to zero via a cancellation between UV
and IR divergences. Since we are interested in the UV divergence
specifically, we can introduce a mass to regulate the IR divergence
separately from the UV divergence. The logarithmic UV divergence in

D=D_{\rm crit}-2\epsilon
spacetime dimensions (given the mostly-plus metric convention and
s_{ij}>0
region) is given by

\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{(\ell^{2})^{D_{\text{crit}}/2}}\Bigg|_{\text{UV}}\!\rightarrow\!\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{(\ell^{2}-m^{2})^{D_{\rm crit}/2}}=\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{\Gamma(\frac{D_{\rm crit}}{2})(m^{2})^{\epsilon}}=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{D_{\rm crit}}{2})\epsilon}+\mathcal{O}(m,\epsilon^{0})\,.

(C.3)

The UV divergence is the residue of the simple pole
\frac{1}{\epsilon}
, with the IR
regulator decoupled into the non-singular terms of order
\epsilon^{\geq 0}
. Thus, the various
orders of
\beta
in the large-
\ell
expansion of (
C.2
)
correspond to logarithmic UV divergences via the mapping

\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}I(\ell)\Bigg|_{\text{UV}}\rightarrow\bar{n}_{D_{\text{crit}}/2}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{D}\ell}{\pi^{D/2}}\frac{1}{\beta^{D_{\rm crit}}(\ell^{2})^{D_{\rm crit}/2}}\Bigg|_{\text{UV}}\to\frac{\bar{n}_{D_{\text{crit}}/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{D_{\rm crit}}{2})\epsilon}\,.

(C.4)

As discussed above, these UV divergences directly correspond to terms
in the expansion of the Schwinger exponent. For example, we can apply

eq.

C.4
to the scalar one-mass triangle
eq.

C.1b
,

I_{3}(k_{1},k_{2})\ \xrightarrow{{\rm UV}}\ \,\begin{array}[]{|*5{>{\displaystyle}c|}}\hline\cr D_{\rm crit}=6&D_{\rm crit}=8&D_{\rm crit}=10&D_{\rm crit}=12&D_{\rm crit}=14\\
\hline\cr&&&&\\[-10.76385pt]
\frac{1}{\epsilon}&-\frac{s_{12}}{24\epsilon}&\frac{s_{12}^{2}}{360\epsilon}&-\frac{s_{12}^{3}}{6720\epsilon}&\frac{s_{12}^{4}}{151200\epsilon}\\[6.45831pt]
\hline\cr\end{array}\;,

(C.5)

which exactly matches the expansion in
section

6.3
. The constructions in

section

6.4
have all been reproduced using this method as well.
Then, as expected from the arguments in
section

C.1
, the

D
-dimensional UV divergences at the residues of the simple poles in

\epsilon=\frac{1}{2}(D_{\rm crit}-D)
are related to the Laurent polynomials via

\coef_{1/\epsilon}\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{A}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{\rm eff}(1,2,3,4)\,\big|_{\rm UV}=\coef_{(-2i\alpha^{\prime}T)^{D_{\rm crit}/2-4}}a^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{{\rm open}}\Big(1,2,3,4;\tau=\frac{T}{\pi}\Big)\,,

(C.6)

which is equivalent to
eq.

6.23
.
The procedure can be just as easily applied to the closed-string
integrands which leads to the expected pattern of UV divergences

\coef_{1/\epsilon}\pi^{-D/2}\widehat{M}^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm eff}}\,\big|_{\rm UV}=\coef_{(4{\alpha^{\prime}}y)^{D_{\rm crit}/2-4}}m^{\textrm{1-loop}}_{4,{\rm closed}}\Big(\Im\tau=\frac{y}{\pi}\Big)\,,

(C.7)

equivalent to
eq.

6.39
.

C.3
Momentum-space and
D_{\text{crit}}\leq 0

For the UV divergences along with higher
\zeta
values, like those
in
eqs.

6.24
,
6.25
and
6.41
, we encounter
powers like
T^{-4},T^{-5},\dots
in the associated Laurent polynomial
eq.

6.8
for open string, and
y^{-4},\dots
in
eq.

6.4
for closed string. By (
C.6
) and
(
C.7
), these UV divergences occur in formally vanishing or
negative spacetime dimensions
D\leq 0
. There are two
points of interest in these cases. The first is that the momentum-space tensor
reduction generates a prefactor
\frac{1}{D(D+2)\dots}
that will
contribute a
\frac{1}{\epsilon}
pole when
D_{\rm crit}=-2N
is even and non-positive. Second, the

\Gamma
-function representation that
eq.

C.4
comes from,

\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{D_{\rm crit}}{2}\right)}\,,

(C.8)

has an infinity-over-infinity ambiguity and thus needs to be properly regulated.
Since the tensor reduction already provides the
\frac{1}{\epsilon}
pole,
the simplest regulation scheme should lead to divergence-free contributions. To
this end, we propose the simple scheme,

\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{D_{\rm crit}}{2}-\epsilon\right)}\,.

(C.9)

For
D_{\rm crit}>0
, the additional
\epsilon
regulator is irrelevant, reducing
the expression back to
eq.

C.4
in the leading-
\epsilon

expansion. However, for
D_{\rm crit}\leq 0
, we instead get

\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{D_{\rm crit}}{2}-\epsilon\right)}\xrightarrow[D_{\rm crit}\text{ even and }D_{\rm crit}\leq 0]{\epsilon\to 0}(-1)^{\frac{D_{\rm crit}}{2}+1}\Gamma\left(\frac{|D_{\rm crit}|}{2}+1\right)\,.

(C.10)

Applying this regularization scheme to the leading terms of
\zeta_{6}
,

\zeta_{7}
, and
\zeta_{8}
in (
6.24
)
exactly lines up with the established correspondence of

eq.

C.6
as desired.

C.4
Integrals with closed formulas

As yet another check, we can extract UV divergence and non-analytic terms from those integrals with closed formulas. The bubble integral
eq.

C.1a
and the one-mass triangle
eq.

C.1b

are given by

\displaystyle I_{2}(k)

\displaystyle=\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{\epsilon\gamma_{E}}\frac{\Gamma(2-\frac{D}{2})\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-1)^{2}}{\Gamma(D-2)}(k^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{D=10-2\epsilon}\;-\frac{k^{6}}{840}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\log\frac{k^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right]-\frac{44k^{6}}{11025}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,,

(C.11a)

\displaystyle I_{3}(k_{1},k_{2})

\displaystyle=\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{\epsilon\gamma_{E}}\frac{\Gamma(3-\frac{D}{2})\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-2)^{2}}{\Gamma(D-3)}(s_{12})^{\frac{D}{2}-3}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{D=10-2\epsilon}\,\frac{s_{12}^{2}}{360}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\log\frac{s_{12}}{\mu^{2}}\right]+\frac{17s_{12}^{2}}{1800}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,.

(C.11b)

Both of the formulas can be found in
Smirnov:2012gma
. On the
other hand, the two-mass triangle
eq.

C.1c
is not a master
integral. It can be reduced to a combination of two bubbles

I_{3}(k_{1},k_{2}{+}k_{3})=\frac{2(D-3)}{D-4}\frac{I_{2}(k_{2}{+}k_{3})-I_{2}(k_{1}{+}k_{2}{+}k_{3})}{s_{12}+s_{13}}\,.

(C.12)

The one-mass box
eq.

C.1d
also has a closed formula
Bern:1993kr
;
Kozlov:2015kol
,

\displaystyle I_{4}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{\epsilon\gamma_{E}}C_{D}}{s_{12}s_{23}}\Bigg[(s_{12})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\mathcal{F}\Big(\frac{s_{13}}{s_{23}}\Big)+(s_{23})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\mathcal{F}\Big(\frac{s_{13}}{s_{12}}\Big)-(s_{123})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\mathcal{F}\Big(\frac{s_{13}s_{123}}{s_{12}s_{23}}\Big)\Bigg],

(C.13)

where

\displaystyle C_{D}=\frac{8\Gamma(3-\frac{D}{2})\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-1)^{2}}{(D-4)^{2}\Gamma(D-3)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(x)=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}}}}F^{{\kern-14.09904pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.14795pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.70906pt{2}\kern 6.14795pt{1}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}}}}F^{{\kern-14.09904pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.14795pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.70906pt{2}\kern 6.14795pt{1}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}}}}F^{{\kern-9.54263pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.83153pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-5.69264pt{2}\kern 3.83153pt{1}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}}}}F^{{\kern-8.4479pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.7368pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-4.59792pt{2}\kern 2.7368pt{1}}}}\bigg[\begin{array}[]{c}1,\,\tfrac{D}{2}{-}2\\
\tfrac{D}{2}{-}1\end{array};-x\bigg]\,.

The UV divergence of the one-mass box at
D=10{-}2\epsilon
is

\displaystyle I_{4}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\xrightarrow{D=10-2\epsilon}-\frac{s_{12}+s_{23}+s_{123}}{120\epsilon}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{0})\,.

(C.16)

The non-analytic terms at
\epsilon^{0}
order contain logarithms and first-order derivatives of hypergeometric functions.
